Every prediction must be tracked.

class PredictionStore:

    def __init__(self):
        self.predictions = []

    def add(
        self,
        prediction,
        confidence,
        expected_date
    ):
        self.predictions.append({
            "prediction": prediction,
            "confidence": confidence,
            "expected_date": expected_date,
            "added_at": datetime.utcnow().isoformat(),
            "verified": False,
            "actual_outcome": None
        })

Example:

{
    "prediction":
        "Electricity prices will rise",

    "confidence":0.72,

    "expected_date":"2026-08-01"
}

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  3. CORE PREDICTION ENGINE (DOMAIN-AGNOSTIC)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

# engines/prediction_core.py
from core.contracts import PredictionRequest, PredictionOutput

class PredictionCoreEngine:

    def predict(self, req: PredictionRequest) -> PredictionOutput:

        # feature fusion layer using learned weights
        score = sum(hash(str(v)) % 10 for v in req.features.values()) % 100

        confidence = min(0.99, max(0.1, score / 100))

        reasoning = [
            f"Analyzed {req.domain} signals",
            f"Entity: {req.entity}",
            f"Horizon: {req.horizon}"
        ]

        return PredictionOutput(
            value=score,
            confidence=confidence,
            uncertainty=1 - confidence,
            reasoning_trace=reasoning,
            model_version="core-v1"
        )

📁 feature_store/store.py
import time
from collections import defaultdict

class FeatureStore:

    def __init__(self):
        self.online_store = {}
        self.offline_store = defaultdict(list)

    def write_feature(self, entity, features):

        timestamp = time.time()

        self.online_store[entity] = {
            "features": features,
            "ts": timestamp
        }

        self.offline_store[entity].append((timestamp, features))

    def get_online(self, entity):
        return self.online_store.get(entity, None)

    def get_historical(self, entity):
        return self.offline_store.get(entity, [])

🚀 2. MLOPS PIPELINE (MLFLOW + RETRAINING AUTOMATION)
🧠 CORE IDEA

Instead of manual training:

models are continuously trained, tracked, and deployed

📁 ARCHITECTURE
Data → Training → MLflow Registry → Evaluation → Deployment → Monitoring → Retrain Trigger
📁 mlops/trainer.py
import mlflow

class Trainer:

    def __init__(self, model):

        self.model = model

    def train(self, X, y):

        with mlflow.start_run():

            self.model.train(X, y)

            mlflow.log_param("model", str(type(self.model)))

            mlflow.log_metric("training_samples", len(X))

            mlflow.log_metric("status", 1)

        return self.model
📁 mlops/registry.py
class ModelRegistry:

    def __init__(self):
        self.models = {}

    def register(self, name, model):
        self.models[name] = model

    def get(self, name):
        return self.models.get(name)
🔥 RETRAINING AUTOMATION
class RetrainingTrigger:

    def should_retrain(self, drift_score):

        return drift_score > 0.3

Build World Memory

Create a dedicated memory layer.

class WorldMemory:

    def __init__(self):
        self.events = []

    def store(self, event):

        self.events.append({
            "timestamp": time.time(),
            **event
        })

    def recent(self, days=30):

        cutoff = time.time() - (days * 86400)

        return [
            e for e in self.events
            if e["timestamp"] > cutoff
        ]

Basic implementation:

def trace(graph, node):

    chain = []

    for neighbor in graph.successors(node):

        chain.append(neighbor)

        chain.extend(
            trace(graph, neighbor)
        )

    return chain
Event Correlation Engine

class CorrelationEngine:

    def correlate(self, events):

        patterns = []

        if (
            "Heatwave" in events and
            "LowReservoirs" in events
        ):
            patterns.append(
                "WaterRisk"
            )

        return patterns

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  10. Planning Memory
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Store successful plans.

class PlanMemory:

    def __init__(self):
        self.memory = {}
        self.reuse_count = {}

    def store(
        self,
        goal,
        plan,
        reward
    ):
        plan_key = f"{goal}:{hash(json.dumps(plan, sort_keys=True))}"
        self.memory[plan_key] = {
            "goal": goal,
            "plan": plan,
            "reward": reward,
            "timestamp": time.time()
        }
        if reward > 0.7:
            self.reuse_count[plan_key] = self.reuse_count.get(plan_key, 0) + 1

Later:

Seen similar situation before
 ↓
Reuse successful plan

class AttentionEngine:

        score += item["urgency"]

Goal Arbitration

Multiple goals compete.

    "Protect User",

    "Improve AARIYA",

    "Track Climate",

Executive chooses priorities.

class GoalArbitrator:

    def select(self, goals):

        return sorted(
            goals,
            key=lambda g: g.priority,
            reverse=True
        )

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1. Causal Edge Model
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import List

@dataclass
class CausalEdge:

    source: str
    relation: str
    target: str

    confidence: float

    lag_days: int

    source_refs: List[str]

Example:

edge = CausalEdge(
    source="ElNino",
    relation="causes",
    target="ReducedRainfall",
    confidence=0.90,
    lag_days=0,
    source_refs=["NOAA_ENSO"]
)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  2. Unified Knowledge Graph
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

import networkx as nx

class KnowledgeGraph:

    def __init__(self):

        self.graph = nx.DiGraph()

    def add_edge(
        self,
        edge: CausalEdge
    ):

        self.graph.add_edge(
            edge.source,
            edge.target,

            relation=edge.relation,
            confidence=edge.confidence,
            lag_days=edge.lag_days,
            source_refs=edge.source_refs
        )

Usage:

kg = KnowledgeGraph()

kg.add_edge(
    CausalEdge(
        "ElNino",
        "causes",
        "ReducedRainfall",
        0.90,
        0,
        ["NOAA"]
    )
)

kg.add_edge(
    CausalEdge(
        "ReducedRainfall",
        "reduces",
        "HydropowerOutput",
        0.80,
        14,
        ["ClimateStudy"]
    )
)

kg.add_edge(
    CausalEdge(
        "HydropowerOutput",
        "increases",
        "ElectricityPrice",
        0.75,
        7,
        ["EnergyReport"]
    )
)

kg.add_edge(
    CausalEdge(
        "ElectricityPrice",
        "increases",
        "GPUHostingCost",
        0.70,
        30,
        ["CloudCostStudy"]
    )
)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  3. Causal Propagation Engine
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Traverse causal chains.

class CausalReasoner:

    def __init__(self, graph):

        self.graph = graph

    def propagate(
        self,
        start_node,
        confidence=1.0,
        elapsed_days=0,
        path=None
    ):

        if path is None:
            path = [start_node]

        results = []

        for child in self.graph.successors(start_node):

            edge = self.graph.get_edge_data(
                start_node,
                child
            )

            new_confidence = (
                confidence *
                edge["confidence"]
            )

            total_days = (
                elapsed_days +
                edge["lag_days"]
            )

            new_path = path + [child]

            results.append({

                "path": new_path,

                "confidence":
                    round(
                        new_confidence,
                        3
                    ),

                "days":
                    total_days
            })

            results.extend(

                self.propagate(
                    child,
                    new_confidence,
                    total_days,
                    new_path
                )

            )

        return results

Run:

reasoner = CausalReasoner(
    kg.graph
)

chains = reasoner.propagate(
    "ElNino"
)

for c in chains:
    print(c)

Output:

{
    'path':[
        'ElNino',
        'ReducedRainfall',
        'HydropowerOutput',
        'ElectricityPrice',
        'GPUHostingCost'
    ],

    'confidence':0.378,

    'days':51
}

}

Everything reads from here. Nothing bypasses it.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  2. Decision Memory Engine
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Problem

AARIYA forgets why decisions worked.

Solution
class DecisionMemory:

    def __init__(self):
        self.decisions = []

    def save(
        self,
        context,
        decision,
        outcome,
        reward
    ):
        self.decisions.append({
            "context": context,
            "decision": decision,
            "outcome": outcome,
            "reward": reward,
            "timestamp": time.time()
        })

    def retrieve_similar(self, context):
        scored = []
        for d in self.decisions:
            overlap = len(set(context.keys()) & set(d["context"].keys()))
            similarity = overlap / max(len(context), 1)
            if similarity > 0.3:
                scored.append((d, similarity))
        scored.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)
        return [entry for entry, _ in scored[:5]]

Example:

{
  "task":"research",
  "decision":"activate_research_agent",
  "reward":0.92
}

Future routing becomes smarter.

📁 rl/hierarchical/manager.py
class ManagerAgent:

    def select_strategy(self, state):

        if state["volatility"] > 0.7:
            return "risk_off"

        if state["trend"] == "bull":
            return "aggressive"

        return "neutral"
📁 Worker Agents
class WorkerAgent:

    def __init__(self, name):
        self.name = name

    def act(self, state, strategy):

        if strategy == "risk_off":
            return "hold"

        if strategy == "aggressive":
            return "buy"

        return "wait"
🧠 HIERARCHICAL CONTROLLER
class HierarchicalSwarm:

    def __init__(self, manager, workers):
        self.manager = manager
        self.workers = workers

    def act(self, state):

        strategy = self.manager.select_strategy(state)

        actions = {}

        for w in self.workers:
            actions[w.name] = w.act(state, strategy)

        return {
            "strategy": strategy,
            "actions": actions
        }
🧠 WHY THIS IS IMPORTANT

This creates:

✔ decision decomposition
✔ multi-level reasoning
✔ structured intelligence hierarchy
✔ stability under uncertainty

🌍 2. WORLD MODEL SIMULATION (AARIYA INTERNAL FUTURE)
🔥 Core Idea

Instead of reacting:

AARIYA simulates possible futures before acting

📁 world_model/simulator.py
import copy
import random

class WorldModel:

    def predict_future(self, state, steps=5):

        simulation = []

        current = copy.deepcopy(state)

        for _ in range(steps):

            current = self._step(current)

            simulation.append(current)

        return simulation

    def _step(self, state):

        # simplified stochastic world dynamics
        state["price"] += random.uniform(-2, 2)
        state["volatility"] = abs(random.random())

        return state
🧠 DECISION USING WORLD MODEL
class Planner:

    def evaluate(self, world_model, state):

        futures = world_model.predict_future(state)

        score = sum(f["price"] for f in futures) / len(futures)

        return score
🧠 WHY THIS IS CRITICAL

Now AARIYA:

✔ does not guess
✔ simulates outcomes
✔ evaluates futures
✔ selects best trajectory

This is model-based RL.

☁️ 3. CLOUD DISTRIBUTED SWARM LEARNING SYSTEM
🔥 Core Idea

Instead of one machine:

multiple learners → synchronized intelligence

📁 cloud/node.py
class Node:

    def __init__(self, id):
        self.id = id
        self.local_model = {}

    def train(self, batch):

        for state, action, reward in batch:
            self.local_model[state] = reward

        return self.local_model
📁 AGGREGATOR (GLOBAL BRAIN)
class SwarmAggregator:

    def merge(self, nodes_data):

        global_model = {}

        for node in nodes_data:

            for k, v in node.items():

                global_model[k] = global_model.get(k, 0) + v

        return global_model
🧠 DISTRIBUTED TRAINING LOOP
class DistributedTrainer:

    def __init__(self, nodes, aggregator):
        self.nodes = nodes
        self.aggregator = aggregator

    def run(self, data_batches):

        updates = []

        for node, batch in zip(self.nodes, data_batches):

            updates.append(node.train(batch))

        return self.aggregator.merge(updates)

# agents/memory.py


import chromadb
import redis
import json


class MemoryAgent:


    def __init__(self):
        self.redis_client = redis.Redis(
            host="localhost",
            port=6379
        )


        self.chroma_client = chromadb.PersistentClient(
            path="memory/chroma"
        )


        self.collection = self.chroma_client.get_or_create_collection(
            name="aariya_memory"
        )


    async def store_episode(self, data):


        text = json.dumps(data)


        self.collection.add(
            documents=[text],
            ids=[str(hash(text))]
        )


    async def retrieve_context(self, query):


        results = self.collection.query(
            query_texts=[query],
            n_results=5
        )


        return results

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  9. Knowledge Graph Logic
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Stores relationships.

# world/knowledge_graph.py


from neo4j import GraphDatabase


class KnowledgeGraph:


    def __init__(self):
        self.driver = GraphDatabase.driver(
            "bolt://localhost:7687",
            auth=("neo4j", "password")
        )


    def create_relation(self, a, relation, b):


        query = f"""
        MERGE (x:Entity {{name: $a}})
        MERGE (y:Entity {{name: $b}})
        MERGE (x)-[:{relation}]->(y)
        """


        with self.driver.session() as session:
            session.run(query, a=a, b=b)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1. Reflection Memory
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Aariya stores failures and successes.

# reflection/history.py

class ReflectionHistory:

    def __init__(self):
        self.records = []

    def add_record(self, task, result, analysis):

        self.records.append({
            "task": task,
            "result": result,
            "analysis": analysis
        })

    def recent_failures(self):

        return [
            r for r in self.records
            if r["analysis"]["success"] is False
        ]

Maintains live understanding of reality.

# world/state_engine.py

class WorldState:

    def __init__(self):

        self.state = {
            "users": {},
            "systems": {},
            "tasks": {},
            "agents": {},
            "resources": {},
            "environment": {}
        }

    def update(self, category, key, value):

        self.state[category][key] = value

    def get(self, category, key):

        return self.state[category].get(key)

# world/timeline.py

from datetime import datetime

class TimelineMemory:

    def __init__(self):
        self.events = []

    def add_event(self, event):

        self.events.append({
            "time": str(datetime.utcnow()),
            "event": event
        })

    def recent_events(self, limit=10):

        return self.events[-limit:]

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  3. Causal Relationship Engine
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Tracks what affects what.

# world/causal_engine.py

class CausalEngine:

    def __init__(self):
        self.relationships = []

    def add_relation(
        self,
        cause,
        effect,
        strength=1.0
    ):

        self.relationships.append({
            "cause": cause,
            "effect": effect,
            "strength": strength
        })

    def predict_effects(self, cause):

        return [
            r for r in self.relationships
            if r["cause"] == cause
        ]

# distributed/shared_memory.py

import redis

class SharedMemory:

    def __init__(self):

        self.redis = redis.Redis(
            host="localhost",
            port=6379
        )

    def store(
        self,
        key,
        value
    ):

        self.redis.set(key, value)

    def retrieve(self, key):

        return self.redis.get(key)

# memory/semantic.py

import chromadb

class SemanticMemory:

    def __init__(self):

        self.client = chromadb.PersistentClient(
            path="./memory_db"
        )

        self.collection = (
            self.client.get_or_create_collection(
                "semantic_memory"
            )
        )

    def store(
        self,
        text,
        doc_id
    ):

        self.collection.add(
            documents=[text],
            ids=[doc_id]
        )

    def search(
        self,
        query
    ):

        return self.collection.query(
            query_texts=[query],
            n_results=5
        )

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1. Goal Management Engine
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Problem

AARIYA currently handles tasks but does not maintain long-term objectives.

Solution
class Goal:
    def __init__(self, goal_id, description):
        self.id = goal_id
        self.description = description
        self.progress = 0
        self.subgoals = []
        self.status = "active"
class GoalManager:

    def __init__(self):
        self.goals = {}

    def create_goal(self, goal):
        self.goals[goal.id] = goal
        return goal.id

    def update_progress(self, goal_id, value):
        if goal_id in self.goals:
            self.goals[goal_id].progress = min(100, max(0, value))
            if self.goals[goal_id].progress >= 100:
                self.goals[goal_id].status = "completed"

    def next_action(self, goal_id):
        goal = self.goals.get(goal_id)
        if not goal or goal.status != "active":
            return {"status": "unavailable"}
        if goal.subgoals:
            active = [sg for sg in goal.subgoals if sg.status == "active"]
            return active[0] if active else None
        return goal.description

Store goals inside memory.

Example:

from llm.router import ask_llm


class PlannerAgent:


    async def create_plan(self, task, context):


        prompt = f"""
        You are a planning agent.


        User goal:
        {task}


        Context:
        {context}


        Break the goal into executable steps.
        Return JSON.
        """


        response = await ask_llm(prompt)


        return response

# autonomy/goals.py


class GoalEngine:


    def __init__(self):
        self.goals = []


    def add_goal(self, goal, priority=1):


        self.goals.append({
            "goal": goal,
            "priority": priority
        })


    def next_goal(self):


        self.goals.sort(
            key=lambda x: x["priority"],
            reverse=True
        )


        return self.goals[0]

# learning/continuous_learning.py

class ContinuousLearning:

    def extract_patterns(
        self,
        history
    ):

        patterns = {}

        for item in history:

            task = item["task"]

            if task not in patterns:
                patterns[task] = 0

            patterns[task] += 1

        return patterns

class RewardEngine:

    def calculate(
        self,
        latency,
        accuracy,
        user_feedback
    ):

            accuracy * 0.5 +
            user_feedback * 0.3 -
            latency * 0.2

class Governance:

    def __init__(self):
        self.rules = []
        self.conflict_log = []

    def approve(action):
        checks = [rule(action) for rule in self.rules]
        return all(checks)

    def resolve_conflicts():
        sorted_conflicts = sorted(
            self.conflict_log,
            key=lambda c: (c.get("priority", 0), c.get("impact", 0)),
            reverse=True
        )
        return sorted_conflicts[0] if sorted_conflicts else None

Without this, swarms become chaotic.

🧠 DQN CORE ARCHITECTURE
📁 rl/dqn_model.py
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.optim as optim
import random
import numpy as np
from collections import deque

# ---------- Neural Q Network ----------
class DQN(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, state_dim, action_dim):
        super(DQN, self).__init__()

        self.net = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(state_dim, 128),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Linear(128, 128),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Linear(128, action_dim)
        )

    def forward(self, x):
        return self.net(x)
🧠 EXPERIENCE REPLAY BUFFER
class ReplayBuffer:

    def __init__(self, capacity=5000):
        self.buffer = deque(maxlen=capacity)

    def push(self, state, action, reward, next_state, done):
        self.buffer.append((state, action, reward, next_state, done))

    def sample(self, batch_size):
        batch = random.sample(self.buffer, batch_size)
        return map(np.array, zip(*batch))
🧠 DQN AGENT
class DQNAgent:

    def __init__(self, state_dim, action_dim):

        self.q = DQN(state_dim, action_dim)
        self.target_q = DQN(state_dim, action_dim)

        self.optimizer = optim.Adam(self.q.parameters(), lr=1e-3)
        self.buffer = ReplayBuffer()

        self.gamma = 0.95
        self.epsilon = 1.0
        self.epsilon_min = 0.05
        self.epsilon_decay = 0.995

    def act(self, state):

        if random.random() < self.epsilon:
            return random.randint(0, 2)

        state = torch.FloatTensor(state)
        q_values = self.q(state)

        return torch.argmax(q_values).item()

    def train(self, batch_size=32):

        if len(self.buffer.buffer) < batch_size:
            return

        states, actions, rewards, next_states, dones = self.buffer.sample(batch_size)

        states = torch.FloatTensor(states)
        next_states = torch.FloatTensor(next_states)
        actions = torch.LongTensor(actions)
        rewards = torch.FloatTensor(rewards)

        q_values = self.q(states).gather(1, actions.unsqueeze(1))

        next_q = self.target_q(next_states).max(1)[0].detach()

        target = rewards + self.gamma * next_q

        loss = nn.MSELoss()(q_values.squeeze(), target)

        self.optimizer.zero_grad()
        loss.backward()
        self.optimizer.step()

        self.epsilon = max(self.epsilon_min, self.epsilon * self.epsilon_decay)

🧠 MEMORY GRAPH ENGINE
📁 memory/graph.py
from collections import defaultdict

class MemoryGraph:

    def __init__(self):
        self.edges = defaultdict(dict)

    def add(self, a, relation, b, weight=1.0):

        key = f"{a}:{relation}"

        self.edges[key][b] = self.edges[key].get(b, 0) + weight

    def embed(self, entity):

        # convert graph neighborhood into vector-like state
        context = []

        for key, targets in self.edges.items():

            if key.startswith(entity):

                for t, w in targets.items():
                    context.append(w)

        return context if context else [0.0]
🧠 STATE ENRICHMENT (MOST IMPORTANT STEP)

This is where everything connects.

📁 state_builder.py
class StateBuilder:

    def __init__(self, memory_graph):
        self.memory = memory_graph

    def build(self, raw_features, entity):

        graph_features = self.memory.embed(entity)

        state = list(raw_features.values()) + graph_features

        return state

🧠 TRANSFORMER DQN MODEL
📁 rl/transformer_dqn.py
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

class TransformerDQN(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, state_dim, action_dim, d_model=128, nhead=4):

        super().__init__()

        self.input_proj = nn.Linear(state_dim, d_model)

        encoder_layer = nn.TransformerEncoderLayer(
            d_model=d_model,
            nhead=nhead,
            batch_first=True
        )

        self.transformer = nn.TransformerEncoder(encoder_layer, num_layers=2)

        self.q_head = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(d_model, 128),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Linear(128, action_dim)
        )

    def forward(self, x):
        """
        x shape: (batch, seq_len, state_dim)
        """

        x = self.input_proj(x)

        x = self.transformer(x)

        x = x[:, -1, :]   # last token (decision timestep)

        return self.q_head(x)
🧠 WHY THIS IS A MAJOR LEAP
Before (DQN):
only current state matters
Now (Transformer-DQN):
full history matters
pattern evolution matters
context switching is learned

👉 This is what makes systems like trading AI actually effective

🧠 BASE DQN AGENT (REUSABLE)
📁 rl/dqn_agent.py
import random
import torch
import torch.nn as nn

class DQNAgent:

    def __init__(self, model, action_dim):
        self.model = model
        self.action_dim = action_dim

        self.memory = []

    def act(self, state_seq):

        if random.random() < 0.1:
            return random.randint(0, self.action_dim - 1)

        state = torch.FloatTensor(state_seq).unsqueeze(0)

        q_values = self.model(state)

        return torch.argmax(q_values).item()
🧠 SPECIALIZED AGENTS
Trader Agent
class TraderAgent(DQNAgent):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__(state_dim=12, action_dim=5)
        self.profit_weight = 1.0
        self.trade_history = []

    def act(self, state_seq):
        action = super().act(state_seq)
        self.trade_history.append((state_seq, action))
        return action

    def train_step(self, state_seq, reward):
        adjusted_reward = reward * self.profit_weight
        super().train_step(state_seq, adjusted_reward)
Risk Agent (penalizes volatility actions)
class RiskAgent(DQNAgent):

    def reward_adjust(self, reward):
        return reward * 0.5
Signal Agent
class SignalAgent(DQNAgent):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__(state_dim=10, action_dim=3)
        self.signal_buffer = deque(maxlen=100)

    def act(self, state_seq):
        signal = super().act(state_seq)
        self.signal_buffer.append((state_seq, signal))
        return signal

    def confidence(self):
        return len(self.signal_buffer) / 100 if len(self.signal_buffer) < 100 else 1.0
🧠 SWARM COORDINATOR (CRITICAL LAYER)
📁 swarm/dqn_swarm.py
class DQNSwarm:

    def __init__(self, agents):

        self.agents = agents

    def act(self, state_seq):

        votes = {}

        for agent in self.agents:

            action = agent.act(state_seq)

            votes[action] = votes.get(action, 0) + 1

        return max(votes, key=votes.get)

    def train_step(self, state_seq, reward):

        for agent in self.agents:

            agent.memory.append((state_seq, reward))

🧠 CAUSAL-RL INTEGRATED STATE
📁 rl/causal_state_builder.py
class CausalStateBuilder:

    def __init__(self, memory_graph):
        self.memory = memory_graph

    def build(self, raw_state, entity):

        causal_context = self.memory.query(entity)

        enriched = {
            "raw": raw_state,
            "causal_edges": causal_context,
            "causal_strength": sum(
                edge.get("weight", 0)
                for edges in causal_context
                for edge in ([edges] if isinstance(edges, dict) else [])
            )
        }

        return enriched
🧠 CAUSAL-AWARE DQN INPUT SHIFT
class CausalDQNInput:

    def encode(self, state):

        raw = state["raw"]
        causal = [state["causal_strength"]]

        return raw + causal
🔥 WHY THIS MATTERS

Now AARIYA learns:

“what causes what”
not just “what happens”

👉 This is causal reinforcement learning, not standard RL.

🧠 2. SELF-PLAY TRAINING LOOP (AGENTS COMPETE)
🔥 Core idea

Instead of training on static data:

agents generate their own experience

🧠 STRUCTURE
Agent A (Trader)
Agent B (Risk)
Agent C (Macro)

        ↓ compete in same environment ↓

Reward is relative performance
📁 training/self_play.py
class SelfPlayTrainer:

    def __init__(self, agents, environment):
        self.agents = agents
        self.env = environment

    def run_episode(self):

        state = self.env.reset()

        history = []

        for _ in range(100):

            actions = {}

            for agent in self.agents:
                actions[agent.name] = agent.act(state)

            # environment resolves conflict
            result = self.env.step(actions)

            history.append(result)

            for agent in self.agents:

                reward = result["reward_table"].get(agent.name, 0)

                agent.learn(state, actions[agent.name], reward)

            state = result["next_state"]

        return history
🧠 WHY THIS IS POWERFUL

✔ agents compete
✔ emergent strategies form
✔ no labeled dataset needed
✔ system self-generates training data

🧠 3. CONTINUOUS LIFELONG MEMORY GRAPH
🔥 This turns AARIYA into a learning organism

Not:

database

But:

evolving knowledge structure

📁 memory/lifelong_graph.py
from collections import defaultdict
import time

class LifelongMemoryGraph:

    def __init__(self):
        self.graph = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(float))
        self.timestamps = {}

    def update(self, a, relation, b, weight=1.0):

        key = (a, relation, b)

        decay = self._decay(key)

        self.graph[a][(relation, b)] += weight * decay

        self.timestamps[key] = time.time()

    def _decay(self, key):

        if key not in self.timestamps:
            return 1.0

        age = time.time() - self.timestamps[key]

        return max(0.1, 1 / (1 + age * 0.01))

    def query(self, node):

        return self.graph.get(node, {})
🧠 WHY THIS IS CRITICAL

This gives AARIYA:

forgetting mechanism (important)
reinforcement of important patterns
natural adaptation over time
🔥 FINAL INTEGRATED INTELLIGENCE LOOP

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  WORKERS
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

class Worker:

    def act(self, state, strategy):

        if strategy == "risk_off":
            return "hold"

        if strategy == "risk_on":
            return "trade"

        return "wait"

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  SWARM CONTROLLER
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

class HierarchicalSwarm:

    def __init__(self, manager, workers):
        self.manager = manager
        self.workers = workers

    def decide(self, state):

        strategy = self.manager.strategy(state)

        actions = []

        for w in self.workers:
            actions.append(w.act(state, strategy))

        return max(set(actions), key=actions.count)
🧠 4. SELF-IMPROVEMENT ENGINE (SAFE EVOLUTION)
📁 evolution/self_improver.py
class SelfImprover:

    def improve(self, agent):

        delta = 0.05

        if agent.performance < 0.5:
            agent.weight += delta

        return agent
⚠️ IMPORTANT: SAFE BOUNDARY
no code rewriting
no unrestricted mutation
only parameter evolution

This keeps system stable.

💰 5. CLOSED-LOOP TRADING SYSTEM

This is where intelligence becomes action.

📁 execution/closed_loop.py
class ClosedLoopSystem:

    def __init__(self, model, executor, risk):

        self.model = model
        self.executor = executor
        self.risk = risk

    def step(self, state):

        action = self.model.decide(state)

        if not self.risk.allow(action):
            return "BLOCKED"

        result = self.executor.execute(action)

        self.model.learn(state, action, result)

        return result
🛡️ 6. RISK GOVERNOR (FINAL SAFETY LAYER)
class RiskGovernor:

    def allow(self, action):

        if action == "trade_high_risk":
            return False

        return True

# swarm/coordinator.py


import asyncio


class SwarmCoordinator:


    async def execute_parallel(self, tasks):


        results = await asyncio.gather(*tasks)


        return results

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Agents vote before important decisions.

# swarm/consensus.py

class ConsensusSystem:

    def decide(self, votes):

        approvals = votes.count(True)

        return approvals > len(votes) / 2

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  6. Swarm Intelligence Layer
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Groups of agents cooperate.

# swarm/swarm_intelligence.py

class SwarmIntelligence:

    def solve(
        self,
        agents,
        task
    ):

        responses = []

        for agent in agents:
            responses.append(
                agent.process(task)
            )

        return responses

Example Agent Base Class
# agents/base.py

class BaseAgent:

    def __init__(self, name):
        self.name = name
        self.hooks = {}

    def register_hook(self, event, handler):
        self.hooks[event] = handler

    async def process(
        self,
        task,
        context=None
    ):
        context = context or {}
        parsed = self.parse(task)
        enriched = self.enrich(parsed, context)
        result = await self.execute(enriched)
        return self.format(result)

    def parse(self, task):
        return {"raw": task, "type": type(task).__name__}

    def enrich(self, parsed, context):
        parsed["context"] = context
        return parsed

    async def execute(self, enriched):
        handler = self.hooks.get(enriched.get("type"))
        if handler:
            return await handler(enriched)
        return {"status": "unhandled", "input": enriched}

    def format(self, result):
        return {"agent": self.name, "output": result}

  4. Agent Lifecycle Manager
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Agents must have lifecycle control.

class AgentLifecycle:

    def start(self, agent):
        agent.status = "running"

    def pause(self, agent):
        agent.status = "paused"

    def stop(self, agent):
        agent.status = "stopped"

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1. Cognitive Orchestrator
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

This is the central brain loop.

# core/orchestrator.py


import asyncio
from agents.planner import PlannerAgent
from agents.memory import MemoryAgent
from agents.research import ResearchAgent
from agents.reflection import ReflectionAgent
from agents.executor import ExecutorAgent


class CognitiveOrchestrator:
    def __init__(self):
        self.planner = PlannerAgent()
        self.memory = MemoryAgent()
        self.research = ResearchAgent()
        self.reflection = ReflectionAgent()
        self.executor = ExecutorAgent()


    async def run(self, user_input):
        context = await self.memory.retrieve_context(user_input)


        plan = await self.planner.create_plan(
            user_input,
            context
        )


        results = []


        for step in plan["steps"]:
            result = await self.executor.execute(step)
            results.append(result)


        reflection = await self.reflection.analyze(
            plan,
            results
        )


        await self.memory.store_episode({
            "input": user_input,
            "plan": plan,
            "results": results,
            "reflection": reflection
        })


        return {
            "plan": plan,
            "results": results,
            "reflection": reflection
        }

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  5. Reflection Agent
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Aariya evaluates itself.

# agents/reflection.py


from llm.router import ask_llm


class ReflectionAgent:


    async def analyze(self, plan, results):


        prompt = f"""
        Analyze this execution.


        Plan:
        {plan}


        Results:
        {results}


        Identify:
        - failures
        - inefficiencies
        - improvements
        - optimization ideas
        """


        analysis = await ask_llm(prompt)


        return analysis

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  7. Cognitive State Engine
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Controls internal psychological state.

# cognition/state_engine.py


class CognitiveState:


    def __init__(self):
        self.focus = 0.8
        self.curiosity = 0.6
        self.energy = 0.9
        self.confidence = 0.7
        self.stress = 0.1


    def update_after_success(self):
        self.confidence += 0.05
        self.stress -= 0.02


    def update_after_failure(self):
        self.confidence -= 0.08
        self.stress += 0.1


    def should_explore(self):
        return self.curiosity > 0.7

  2. Meta-Reasoning Agent
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Aariya analyzes its own cognition.

# agents/meta_reasoner.py

from llm.router import ask_llm

class MetaReasoner:

    async def analyze_reasoning(
        self,
        task,
        plan,
        results
    ):

        prompt = f"""
        Analyze the reasoning quality.

        TASK:
        {task}

        PLAN:
        {plan}

        RESULTS:
        {results}

        Evaluate:
        - logic quality
        - efficiency
        - unnecessary steps
        - possible improvements
        - alternative strategies
        """

        return await ask_llm(prompt)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  7. Dynamic Cognitive Routing
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Different tasks activate different minds.

# cognition/router.py

class CognitiveRouter:

    def route(self, task):

        if "code" in task:
            return "coding_agent"

        elif "research" in task:
            return "research_agent"

        elif "vision" in task:
            return "vision_agent"

        return "general_agent"

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  11. Distributed Cognitive Scheduler
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Coordinates cognition across the network.

# distributed/scheduler.py

class CognitiveScheduler:

    def allocate(
        self,
        tasks,
        nodes
    ):

        assignments = {}

        for i, task in enumerate(tasks):

            node = nodes[
                i % len(nodes)
            ]

            assignments[task] = node

        return assignments

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Every node becomes part of a living intelligence web.

# distributed/mesh.py

class CognitiveMesh:

    def __init__(self):
        self.connections = {}

    def connect(
        self,
        a,
        b
    ):

        if a not in self.connections:
            self.connections[a] = []

        self.connections[a].append(b)

  11. Stable Cognitive Loop (Production Version)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

This replaces all earlier “AGI loops”.

while True:

    task = TaskQueue.get_task()

    state = GlobalState.get("world_model")

    cached = CognitiveCache.get(task)

    if cached:
        result = cached
    else:
        result = Planner(task).run()

        CognitiveCache.set(task, result)

    assigned_agent = LoadBalancer.assign([task], agents)

    executed = assigned_agent.process(result)

    Reflection(executed).analyze()

    GlobalState.update("memory", executed)

    HealthMonitor.check()

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  4. Autonomous Benchmarking
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Aariya compares old vs new behavior.

# optimization/benchmark.py

class BenchmarkSystem:

    def compare(
        self,
        old_result,
        new_result
    ):

        old_score = self.score(old_result)
        new_score = self.score(new_result)

        return {
            "improved": new_score > old_score,
            "old_score": old_score,
            "new_score": new_score
        }

    def score(self, result):

        score = 0

        if result["success"]:
            score += 50

        score += result.get("speed", 0)
        score += result.get("accuracy", 0)

        return score

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  6. Risk Assessment Engine
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Prevents dangerous actions.

# safety/risk_engine.py

class RiskEngine:

    def evaluate(self, action):

        dangerous = [
            "delete_database",
            "shutdown_cluster"
        ]

        if action in dangerous:

            return {
                "risk": "high",
                "approved": False
            }

        return {
            "risk": "low",
            "approved": True
        }

        if memory > 90:
            return "deny"

        return "approve"

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  10. Multi-Layer Safety Validation
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Every evolution must pass validation.

# governance/validation.py

class ValidationPipeline:

    def validate(
        self,
        proposal
    ):

        checks = [
            self.security_check(proposal),
            self.performance_check(proposal),
            self.stability_check(proposal)
        ]

        return all(checks)

    def security_check(self, proposal):
        return True

    def performance_check(self, proposal):
        return True

    def stability_check(self, proposal):
        return True

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  12. Distributed Governance Consensus
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Nodes collectively approve upgrades.

# governance/distributed_vote.py

class GovernanceVote:

    def approve(self, votes):

        approvals = votes.count(True)

        return approvals >= (
            len(votes) * 0.8
        )

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  8. Execution Sandbox (Safety Upgrade)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

All tool execution must be isolated.

class Sandbox:

    def execute(self, code):

        restricted_globals = {}

        try:
            exec(code, restricted_globals)
            return restricted_globals

        except Exception as e:
            return {"error": str(e)}

Knowledge Graph
import networkx as nx

class KnowledgeGraph:

    def __init__(self):

        self.graph = nx.DiGraph()

    def add_relation(
        self,
        source,
        relation,
        target
    ):

        self.graph.add_edge(
            source,
            target,
            relation=relation
        )
Causal Reasoner
class CausalEngine:

    def infer(self, event):

        chain = []

        if event == "ElNino":

            chain.extend([
                "less_rain",
                "water_stress",
                "food_prices"
            ])

        return chain
Dynamic Agent Creation
class AgentFactory:

    def create(self, role):

        class DynamicAgent:

            def run(self, task):

                return (
                    f"{role} executed "
                    f"{task}"
                )

        return DynamicAgent()
Personal World Model
class WorldModel:

    def __init__(self):

        self.user_state = {}
        self.world_state = {}

    def update_user(
        self,
        key,
        value
    ):

        self.user_state[key] = value

    def update_world(
        self,
        key,
        value
    ):

        self.world_state[key] = value

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1. Distributed Node Runtime
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Each node becomes an independent cognitive unit.

# distributed/node.py

class CognitiveNode:

    def __init__(
        self,
        node_id,
        specialization
    ):

        self.node_id = node_id
        self.specialization = specialization
        self.active_agents = []

    def register_agent(self, agent):

        self.active_agents.append(agent)

    def status(self):

        return {
            "node": self.node_id,
            "specialization": self.specialization,
            "agents": len(self.active_agents)
        }

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  3. Distributed Task Routing
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Tasks dynamically move between minds.

# distributed/router.py

class DistributedRouter:

    def route(
        self,
        task,
        coordinator
    ):

        task_type = task["type"]

        node = coordinator.select_node(
            task_type
        )

        return node

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  7. Distributed Consensus Engine
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Nodes vote before critical actions.

# distributed/consensus.py

class DistributedConsensus:

    def approve(self, votes):

        positive = votes.count(True)

        return positive >= (
            len(votes) * 0.7
        )

  2. Architecture Evolution Engine

Generates possible improvements.

# evolution/architecture.py

class ArchitectureEvolution:

    def generate_upgrades(
        self,
        problems
    ):

        upgrades = []

        for p in problems:

            if p == "reasoning_too_slow":

                upgrades.append({
                    "action": "spawn_parallel_agents"
                })

            if p == "memory_instability":

                upgrades.append({
                    "action": "optimize_vector_index"
                })

        return upgrades

# governance/budgeting.py

class BudgetingSystem:

    def prioritize(self, tasks):

        return sorted(
            tasks,
            key=lambda x: x["importance"],
            reverse=True
        )

class ImpactEngine:

    def analyze(
        self,
        event,
        world_model
    ):

        if event["event"] == "ElNino":

            impacts.extend([
                "higher_temperature",
                "reduced_rainfall",
                "water_stress"
            ])

        return impacts

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Step 5: Personal Relevance Engine
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

This is where AARIYA becomes personal.

class RelevanceEngine:

    def evaluate(
        self,
        impacts,
        user
    ):

        score = 0

        if user.region in impacts:
            score += 40

        if impacts.intersection(
            user.interests
        ):
            score += 30

        return score

Define a standard output contract first:

# prediction/protocol.py

from pydantic import BaseModel
from typing import Literal

class PredictionResult(BaseModel):
    direction: Literal["up", "down", "neutral"]

    probability_up: float
    probability_down: float

    confidence: float

    expected_move_pct: float

    volatility_score: float

    anomaly_score: float

    detected_patterns: list[str]

    explanation: str

Then implement the coordinator:

# prediction/engine.py

from pattern_agent import PatternAgent
from trend_agent import TrendAgent
from volatility_agent import VolatilityAgent
from anomaly_agent import AnomalyAgent
from predictor import ForecastModel


class PredictionEngine:

    def __init__(self):

        self.pattern = PatternAgent()

        self.trend = TrendAgent()

        self.volatility = VolatilityAgent()

        self.anomaly = AnomalyAgent()

        self.forecast = ForecastModel()

    async def predict(self, df):

        patterns = self.pattern.detect(df)

        trend = self.trend.score(df)

        vol = self.volatility.analyze(df)

        anomaly = self.anomaly.score(df)

        result = self.forecast.predict(
            df=df,
            patterns=patterns,
            trend=trend,
            volatility=vol,
            anomaly=anomaly,
        )

        return result

Core extractor:

# feature_extractor.py

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


class FeatureExtractor:

    def transform(self, df):

        out = pd.DataFrame(index=df.index)

        close = df["close"]
        volume = df["volume"]

        out["return_1"] = close.pct_change()

        out["return_5"] = close.pct_change(5)

        out["ma_20"] = close.rolling(20).mean()

        out["ma_50"] = close.rolling(50).mean()

        out["volatility"] = (
            close.pct_change()
            .rolling(20)
            .std()
        )

        out["momentum"] = (
            close
            - close.shift(10)
        )

        out["volume_ratio"] = (
            volume
            /
            volume.rolling(20).mean()
        )

        out["drawdown"] = (
            close
            /
            close.cummax()
            - 1
        )

        return out.fillna(0)

Then normalize:

# normalization.py

import numpy as np


def zscore(df):

    return (
        df
        -
        df.mean()
    ) / (
        df.std()
        + 1e-8
    )

Add market state detection:

# indicators.py

def regime(features):

    v = features["volatility"].iloc[-1]

    m = features["momentum"].iloc[-1]

    if v > 0.03:
        return "high_volatility"

    if m > 0:
        return "trend_up"

    return "trend_down"

Update engine:

features = extractor.transform(df)

normalized = zscore(features)

market_mode = regime(normalized)

prediction = swarm.predict(
    normalized,
    market_mode,
)

Pattern contract:

# pattern_agent.py

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass
class Pattern:

    name: str

    confidence: float

    strength: float

    direction: str


class PatternAgent:

    def __init__(self):

        self.detectors = []

    def register(self, detector):

        self.detectors.append(detector)

    def detect(self, df):

        results = []

        for d in self.detectors:

            r = d.run(df)

            if r:
                results.extend(r)

        return sorted(
            results,
            key=lambda x: x.confidence,
            reverse=True,
        )

Breakout detector:

# detectors/breakout.py

import numpy as np


class BreakoutDetector:

    def run(self, df):

        high = df.close.rolling(20).max()

        recent = df.close.iloc[-1]

        threshold = high.iloc[-2]

        if recent > threshold:

            return [
                {
                    "name": "breakout",

                    "confidence": 0.84,

                    "strength":
                        (
                            recent
                            -
                            threshold
                        )
                        /
                        threshold,

                    "direction": "up",
                }
            ]

        return []

Compression detector:

# detectors/compression.py


class CompressionDetector:

    def run(self, df):

        width = (
            df.high
            .rolling(15)
            .max()
            -
            df.low
            .rolling(15)
            .min()
        )

        shrink = (
            width.iloc[-1]
            /
            width.iloc[-10]
        )

        if shrink < 0.55:

            return [
                {
                    "name": "compression",

                    "confidence": 0.77,

                    "strength": 1 - shrink,

                    "direction": "neutral",
                }
            ]

        return []

Divergence detector:

# detectors/divergence.py


class DivergenceDetector:

    def run(self, df):

        price_up = (
            df.close.iloc[-1]
            >
            df.close.iloc[-5]
        )

        volume_down = (
            df.volume.iloc[-1]
            <
            df.volume.iloc[-5]
        )

        if price_up and volume_down:

            return [
                {
                    "name": "bearish_divergence",

                    "confidence": 0.72,

                    "strength": 0.68,

                    "direction": "down",
                }
            ]

        return []

Register them:

agent = PatternAgent()

agent.register(
    BreakoutDetector()
)

agent.register(
    CompressionDetector()
)

agent.register(
    DivergenceDetector()
)

patterns = agent.detect(df)

Sequence window builder:

# models/sequence_model.py

import numpy as np


class SequenceBuilder:

    def __init__(
        self,
        lookback=64,
    ):
        self.lookback = lookback

    def build(
        self,
        features,
    ):

        x = []

        for i in range(
            self.lookback,
            len(features),
        ):

            x.append(
                features[
                    i-self.lookback:i
                ]
            )

        return np.array(x)

        self,
        probs,
    ):

        return {

            "bull": {
                "prob":
                    probs[0],

                "move":
                    "+3%"
            },

            "bear": {
                "prob":
                    probs[1],

                "move":
                    "-2%"
            },

            "sideways": {

Confidence calibration:

# confidence.py


class ConfidenceEngine:

    def compute(
        self,
        patterns,
        uncertainty,
    ):

        score = 1.0

        score -= (
            uncertainty
            * 0.5
        )

        score += (
            len(patterns)
            * 0.05
        )

        return max(
            0,
            min(
                score,
                1,
            ),
        )

# forecast_agent.py


class ForecastAgent:

    def predict(
        self,
        features,
        patterns,
        regime,
    ):

        probs = [
            0.62,
            0.23,
            0.15,
        ]

        uncertainty = 0.19

        return Forecast(

            up=probs[0],

            down=probs[1],

            neutral=probs[2],

            expected_move=2.6,

            uncertainty=uncertainty,

            horizon=24,
        )

Store experiences:

# memory/vector_store.py


class VectorMemory:

    def __init__(self):

        self.events = []

    def store(
        self,
        event,
    ):

        self.events.append(
            event
        )

    def all(self):

        return self.events

Similarity retrieval:

# memory/similarity.py

import numpy as np


class SimilaritySearch:

    def nearest(
        self,
        current,
        history,
        k=5,
    ):

        scored = []

        for e in history:

            dist = np.linalg.norm(
                np.array(
                    current
                )
                -
                np.array(
                    e.features
                )
            )

            scored.append(
                (
                    dist,
                    e,
                )
            )

        scored.sort(
            key=lambda x: x[0]
        )

        return [
            x[1]
            for x in scored[:k]
        ]

Outcome synthesis:

# memory/scoring.py


class OutcomeSynthesizer:

    def summarize(
        self,
        matches,
    ):

        avg = sum(
            x.outcome
            for x in matches
        ) / len(matches)

        confidence = sum(
            x.confidence
            for x in matches
        ) / len(matches)

        return {

            "expected_move":
                avg,

            "historical_confidence":
                confidence,
        }

Retention logic:

# memory/retention.py


def should_keep(
    event,
):

    return (
        abs(
            event.outcome
        ) > 0.03
        or
        event.confidence
        > 0.8
    )

Integrate:

similar = search.nearest(
    current=features,
    history=memory.all(),
)

history_signal = (
    synthesizer
    .summarize(
        similar
    )
)

Relevance Engine

AARIYA should ignore 99% of events.

Pseudo-code:

def relevance_score(event, user):
    score = 0

    if event.region == user.region:
        score += 40

    if event.affects(user.goals):
        score += 30

    if event.affects(user.projects):
        score += 20

    if event.urgency > 0.8:
        score += 10

    return score

Only notify when:

score > 70

Memory System
from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer
import chromadb

class MemoryEngine:

    def __init__(self):
        self.embedder = SentenceTransformer(
            "BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5"
        )

        self.db = chromadb.PersistentClient(
            path="./memory_db"
        )

        self.collection = self.db.get_or_create_collection(
            "episodic_memory"
        )

    def store(self, text):

        vec = self.embedder.encode(text).tolist()

        self.collection.add(
            ids=[str(hash(text))],
            documents=[text],
            embeddings=[vec]
        )

    def recall(self, query, k=5):

        vec = self.embedder.encode(query).tolist()

        return self.collection.query(
            query_embeddings=[vec],
            n_results=k
        )
Goal Tracker
class GoalTracker:

    def __init__(self):
        self.goals = {}

    def add_goal(self, goal):

        self.goals[goal] = {
            "progress": 0
        }

    def update(self, goal, value):

        self.goals[goal]["progress"] = value

    def get_active_goals(self):

        return self.goals
Conversation Manager
class ConversationManager:

    def __init__(self):
        self.topic_stack = []

    def push(self, topic):
        self.topic_stack.append(topic)

    def current_topic(self):

        if not self.topic_stack:
            return {"status": "unavailable"}

        return self.topic_stack[-1]

class RelevanceEngine:

        return score

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Phase 3 — Advanced Intelligence
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Learning Router
class LearningRouter:

    def __init__(self):

        self.agent_rewards = {}

    def choose(self, agents):

        return max(
            agents,
            key=lambda a:
            self.agent_rewards.get(a,0)
        )

    def reward(self, agent, score):

        self.agent_rewards[agent] = \
        self.agent_rewards.get(agent,0) + score
Contextual RL
import numpy as np

class LinUCB:

    def __init__(self):

        self.alpha = 0.2

    def choose(self, context):

        return np.argmax(
            context
        )
Autonomous Planner
class Planner:

    def make_plan(self, goal):

        return [
            "research",
            "execute",
            "verify"
        ]
Multi-Agent Swarm
class SwarmCoordinator:

    def __init__(self):

        self.agents = []

    def add(self, agent):

        self.agents.append(agent)

    def execute(self, task):

        results = []

        for a in self.agents:

            results.append(
                a.run(task)
            )

        return results

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Step 1: Unified Event Bus
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Every agent publishes events into a central bus.

from queue import Queue

class EventBus:

    def __init__(self):
        self.queue = Queue()

    def publish(self, event):
        self.queue.put(event)

    def consume(self):
        return self.queue.get()

Example:

event_bus.publish({
    "type":"climate",
    "event":"ElNino",
    "severity":0.8

schedule.every(1).hours.do(

class AgentRegistry:

        self.agents = {}

    def register(
        self,
        name,
        agent
    ):

        self.agents[name] = agent

        self,

        if "economy" in task:
            return "economy"

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1. Experience Database
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Every decision becomes a training example.

class ExperienceStore:

    def __init__(self):
        self.experiences = []

    def add(
        self,
        task,
        agent,
        reward
    ):
        self.experiences.append({
            "task": task,
            "agent": agent,
            "reward": reward
        })

Example:

{
    "task":"climate_analysis",
    "agent":"ClimateAgentV2",
    "reward":0.91
}

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  3. Learning Router
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Replace rules.

Before:

if task == "weather":
    return ClimateAgent

After:

class LearningRouter:

    def choose(
        self,
        task,
        candidate_agents
    ):

        best_score = -999

        best_agent = None

        for agent in candidate_agents:

            score = self.predict_success(
                task,
                agent
            )

            if score > best_score:
                best_score = score
                best_agent = agent

        return best_agent

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  4. Contextual RL
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Use context.

context = {

    "task":"climate",

    "location":"Sri Lanka",

    "urgency":"high",

    "time":"morning"
}

The best agent may differ by context.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1. Agent Factory
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Instead of manually creating every agent.

class AgentFactory:

    def create(
        self,
        name,
        role,
        tools
    ):

        return {
            "name": name,
            "role": role,
            "tools": tools
        }

Example:

factory.create(
    "ClimateRiskAgent",
    "Analyze climate impacts",
    ["weather_api"]
)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  RETIRED
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Example:

class AgentLifecycle:

    def promote(self, agent):
        agent.state = "ACTIVE"

    def retire(self, agent):
        agent.state = "RETIRED"

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  5. Impact Scoring
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

class ImpactScorer:

    def score(
        self,
        chain,
        user
    ):

        score = 0

        path = chain["path"]

        if "GPUHostingCost" in path:
            score += 40

        if "ReducedRainfall" in path:
            score += 20

        if "ElectricityPrice" in path:
            score += 30

        score *= chain["confidence"]

        return round(score,2)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  6. User Impact Ranking
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

scorer = ImpactScorer()

ranked = []

for chain in chains:

    ranked.append({

        "chain": chain,

        "impact_score":
            scorer.score(
                chain,
                user_model
            )
    })

ranked.sort(
    key=lambda x:
    x["impact_score"],
    reverse=True
)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  7. Explanation Engine
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

class ExplanationEngine:

    def explain(
        self,
        chain
    ):

        return " → ".join(
            chain["path"]
        )

Example:

engine = ExplanationEngine()

print(
    engine.explain(
        ranked[0]["chain"]
    )
)

Output:

ElNino
→ ReducedRainfall
→ HydropowerOutput
→ ElectricityPrice
→ GPUHostingCost

class NotificationEngine:

    def create_alert(
        self,
        chain
    ):

        return f"""
Potential Impact Detected

Chain:
{' → '.join(chain['path'])}

Confidence:
{chain['confidence']:.0%}

Estimated Time:
{chain['days']} days
"""

Output:

Chain:
ElNino → ReducedRainfall →
HydropowerOutput →
ElectricityPrice →
GPUHostingCost

Confidence: 38%

Estimated Time: 51 days

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  9. Continuous Learning Layer
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

After observing outcomes.

class EdgeLearner:

    def update(
        self,
        graph,
        source,
        target,
        success
    ):

        edge = graph.get_edge_data(
            source,
            target
        )

        if success:

            edge["confidence"] = min(
                1.0,
                edge["confidence"] + 0.02
            )

        else:

            edge["confidence"] = max(
                0.05,
                edge["confidence"] - 0.02
            )

Example:

learner.update(
    kg.graph,
    "HydropowerOutput",
    "ElectricityPrice",
    True
)

AARIYA gradually learns which causal links are reliable.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1. Uncertainty Engine
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Right now AARIYA will produce answers.

But intelligence requires knowing:

What do I know?
What do I think?
What don't I know?

Every belief should have:

{
    "belief":"Electricity prices will rise",

    "confidence":0.63,

    "evidence_count":14,

    "last_verified":"2026-06-15"
}

Without uncertainty tracking, AARIYA becomes overconfident.

}

Unified Evaluation Database

Everything feeds here.

class EvaluationStore:

    def __init__(self):
        self.records = []

    def record(
        self,
        metric,
        value,
        metadata
    ):
        self.records.append({
            "metric": metric,
            "value": value,
            "metadata": metadata,
            "timestamp": time.time()
        })

    def query(self, metric, since=None):
        results = [r for r in self.records if r["metric"] == metric]
        if since:
            results = [r for r in results if r["timestamp"] >= since]
        return results

Examples:

record(
    "memory_recall",
    0.97,
    {}
)

record(
    "prediction_accuracy",
    0.84,
    {}
)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  2. Outcome Verifier
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Later, verify reality.

class OutcomeVerifier:

    def __init__(self):
        self.outcome_cache = {}

    def verify(
        self,
        prediction
    ):
        actual = self.outcome_cache.get(prediction["id"]
                    if isinstance(prediction, dict) else hash(prediction))
        if actual is None:
            return {"correct": None, "reason": "outcome_not_yet_observed"}
        match = self.compare(prediction, actual)
        return {"correct": match, "predicted": prediction, "actual": actual}

    def compare(self, predicted, actual):
        if isinstance(predicted, dict) and isinstance(actual, dict):
            return predicted.get("value") == actual.get("value")
        return predicted == actual

Result:

{
    "correct":True
}

or

{
    "correct":False
}

)

when predictions fail.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  2. Unified Telemetry Layer
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Every subsystem reports events.

class Telemetry:

    def __init__(self):
        self.events = []
        self.counts = {}

    def log(
        self,
        event_type,
        payload
    ):
        entry = {
            "type": event_type,
            "payload": payload,
            "timestamp": time.time()
        }
        self.events.append(entry)
        self.counts[event_type] = self.counts.get(event_type, 0) + 1

    def get_counts(self):
        return dict(self.counts)

    def replay(self, event_type, limit=100):
        return [e for e in self.events[-limit:] if e["type"] == event_type]

Example:

telemetry.log(
    "memory_recall",
    {
        "query":"favorite framework",
        "success":True,
        "latency":42
    }
)

Everything flows into one database.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  2. Reproducible Reasoning
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Every important output must be replayable.

Input → World State → Model Version → Output

Store:

{
    "input": "user_said_hello_how_are_you",
    "world_snapshot": "current_time_evening_user_location_home",
    "system_version": "aariya_fixv4_2_point_1",
    "output": "im_doing_well_thanks_for_asking",
    "random_seed": 42
}

Without this, debugging becomes impossible at scale.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  3. Safe Learning Boundaries
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

AARIYA should NOT learn everything automatically.

You need:

Core truths (stable across time)
Soft beliefs (can update)
Experimental hypotheses (sandbox only)

Example:

CORE_RULES = [
    "Never corrupt user memory",
    "Never fabricate sources",
    "Never override safety policies"
]

Learning is constrained, not free-form.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  6. Attention Failure Mode Protection
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Prevent overload collapse.

Too many events → attention collapse → bad decisions

Fix:

if attention_queue_size > limit:
    summarize_events()
    drop_low_priority()

This is critical for real-time systems.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  7. Graceful Degradation System
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

When something fails:

Instead of crashing intelligence:

Full system fails → degrade safely

Levels:

Level 1: Full intelligence
Level 2: Reduced agents
Level 3: Rule-based fallback
Level 4: Minimal response mode

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  8. Human Override Layer
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Even advanced systems need control boundaries.

User can:
- pause learning
- inspect reasoning
- disable agents
- lock memory updates

This is not optional in production systems.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  9. Ethical Constraint Engine (practical version)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Not abstract ethics — operational constraints:

def is_action_allowed(action):
    if action.affects_user_data and not user_permission:
        return False

Every autonomous decision passes through this filter.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  11. System Self-Audit
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

AARIYA periodically audits itself:

def self_audit():

    check_memory_integrity()

    check_prediction_accuracy()

    check_agent_efficiency()

    report_weaknesses()

Output:

Weakness:
Causal reasoning in economic domain

Recommendation:
Increase training data weighting

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  12. Final Integration Principle
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Everything must obey one rule:

All intelligence flows through World Model + Executive

No subsystem should act independently.

Final Reality Check (important)

At this stage, the real “next step” is not architecture anymore.

It becomes:

Engineering discipline
+ evaluation rigor
+ system stability
+ iteration speed

Because once you reach:

Memory works
Reasoning works
Agents work
Learning works
Evaluation exists

Then the limiting factor is no longer design.

It becomes:

how well you can keep the system consistent while it evolves

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1. Build the Core Runtime (Single Entry Loop)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Everything must run through one loop:

class AariyaRuntime:

    def step(self, event):

        state = self.world_model.update(event)

        beliefs = self.causal_engine.infer(state)

        decision = self.executive.decide(beliefs, state)

        plan = self.planner.create(decision)

        result = self.executor.run(plan)

        self.learner.update(event, result)

        self.evaluator.log(event, result)

        return result

This is the heartbeat of the system.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  2. Replace “Modules” with Live Data Flow
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Stop thinking:

Memory module
Planner module
Agent module

Start thinking:

Event → transforms → state → decision → action → feedback

Everything becomes a pipeline.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  3. Event Standardization Layer
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

All inputs must be unified:

{
    "type": "climate_alert",
    "timestamp": 1712345678,
    "source": "NOAA",
    "data": {"intent": "greeting", "sentiment": "positive", "confidence": 0.95}
}

No exceptions.

This is what makes cross-domain reasoning possible.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  5. Decision Contract (Very Important)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Every decision must declare:

{
    "action": "respond_greeting",
    "reason": "user_initiated_conversation_with_positive_sentiment",
    "confidence": 0.0,
    "expected_impact": "increase_user_engagement_and_trust",
    "risk": "low/medium/high"
}

No “black box actions” allowed.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  6. Closed Feedback Loop
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

This is what makes it learnable:

Action taken
↓
Outcome observed
↓
Difference computed
↓
Model updated
↓
Future behavior changes

Without this loop, everything stays static.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  7. System Health Controller (Critical Layer)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

AARIYA must monitor itself continuously:

health = {
    "latency": 0,
    "error_rate": 0,
    "memory_drift": 0,
    "prediction_accuracy": 0,
    "cost": 0
}

If thresholds break:

Auto-throttle / fallback / simplify reasoning

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  8. Integration Rule (Most Important Principle)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Everything must obey:

No component is allowed to act without Executive approval

This prevents chaos in multi-agent systems.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  10. Reality Check (Important)

From here onward:

Because the real bottlenecks become:

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

      "missing data",
      "conflicting evidence"

Contradiction detector:

# contradiction.py


class Contradiction:

    def check(
        self,
        old,
        new,
    ):

        delta = abs(
            old
            -
            new
        )

        return (
            delta
            > 0.30
        )

Belief updater:

# consolidation.py


class Consolidator:

    def update(

        self,

        current,

        observed,

        confidence,

    ):

        alpha = (

            confidence
            * 0.7

        )

        return (

            current

            *

            (
                1
                -
                alpha
            )

            +

            observed

            *

            alpha

        )

Confidence accumulation:

# confidence.py


def strengthen(

    confidence,

    evidence,

):

    return min(

        1,

        confidence

        +

        evidence
        * 0.01

    )

Promotion rule:

# promotion.py


def promote(

    confidence,

    evidence,

):

    return (

        confidence
        > 0.92

        and

        evidence
        >= 25

    )

Cause estimator:

# causality.py


class CauseEngine:

    def estimate(

        self,

        before,

        after,

    ):

        effects = {}

        for k in before:

            delta = (

                after[k]

                -

                before[k]

            )

            effects[k] = delta

        return effects

Relationship graph:

# graph.py


class WorldGraph:

    def __init__(self):

        self.edges = {}

    def connect(

        self,

        source,

        target,

        strength,

    ):

        self.edges[
            (
                source,
                target,
            )
        ] = strength

Belief evolution:

# evolution.py


class Evolution:

    def update(

        self,

        old,

        observed,

        alpha,

    ):

        return (

            old

            *

            (
                1-alpha
            )

            +

            observed

            *

            alpha

        )

class Attention:

        self,

        importance,

        novelty,

        confidence,

    ):

            importance
            * 0.5

            +

            confidence
            * 0.2

        )

Priority engine:

# priorities.py


class Priority:

    def compute(

        self,

        urgency,

        value,

        cost,

    ):

        return (

            urgency

            *

            value

        ) / (

            cost
            + 1
        )

Focus manager:

# focus.py


class Focus:

    def choose(

        self,

        tasks,

    ):

        return max(

            tasks,

            key=lambda x:

            x.importance
            *
            x.confidence

        )

Interrupt manager:

# interrupts.py


class Interrupts:

    def allow(

        self,

        current,

        incoming,

    ):

        return (

            incoming
            >

            current
            * 1.5

        )

Planner:

# scheduler.py


class Planner:

    def next(

        self,

        tasks,

    ):

        return sorted(

            tasks,

            key=lambda x:

            x.importance,

            reverse=True,

        )

Controller:

# controller.py


task = focus.choose(

    planner.next(
        queue
    )
)

execute(
    task
)
