Continuity scorer:

# continuity.py


class Continuity:

    def measure(

        self,

        previous,

        current,

    ):

        overlap = 0

        total = len(current)

        for k in current:

            if (
                k
                in previous
            ):

                overlap += 1

        return (

            overlap

            /

            max(
                total,
                1,
            )

        )

Priority aging:

# aging.py


import math


def age(

    score,

    hours,

):

    return (

        score

        *

        math.exp(

            -hours
            / 72

        )

    )

Context compression:

# compression.py


class Compress:

    def summarize(

        self,

        states,

    ):

        return {

            "dominant":

            max(

                states,

                key=lambda x:

                x.confidence,

            )
        }

State store:

# state_store.py


class TemporalStore:

    def __init__(self):

        self.history = []

    def save(

        self,

        state,

    ):

        self.history.append(
            state
        )

Expression mapper:

# expression.py


class Expression:

    def choose(

        self,

        confidence,

        uncertainty,

    ):

        if uncertainty > 0.7:

            return "thinking"

        if confidence > 0.8:

            return "focused"

        return "neutral"

Attention model:

# attention.py


class Attention:

    def allocate(

        self,

        active_goal,

        interruptions,

    ):

        score = (

            active_goal

            -

            interruptions
        )

        return max(
            0,
            score,
        )

Voice timing:

# voice.py


class Voice:

    def pace(

        self,

        confidence,

    ):

        if confidence < 0.4:

            return "slower"

        return "normal"

Behavior timing:

# timing.py


class Timing:

    def delay(

        self,

        uncertainty,

    ):

        return (

            uncertainty
            * 500

        )

Gesture selection:

# gestures.py


class Gesture:

    def select(

        self,

        certainty,

    ):

        if certainty > 0.8:

            return "direct"

        return "minimal"

class Permission:

        self,

        risk,

        confidence,

    ):

            confidence > 0.80

            and

            risk < 0.30

        )

class Preconditions:

        self,

        conditions,

    ):

        return all(
            conditions

Bus:

# bus.py


class CognitiveBus:

    def __init__(self):

        self.subscribers = []

    def publish(

        self,

        event,

    ):

        for s in self.subscribers:

            s.receive(
                event
            )

    def subscribe(

        self,

        system,

    ):

        self.subscribers.append(
            system
        )

Shared state:

# registry.py


class Registry:

    def __init__(self):

        self.state = {}

    def update(

        self,

        key,

        value,

    ):

        self.state[key] = value

    def snapshot(

        self,

    ):

        return self.state.copy()

Health monitor:

# profiler.py


class Health:

    def score(

        self,

        metrics,

    ):

        return (

            metrics.accuracy
            * 0.35

            +

            metrics.consistency
            * 0.30

            +

            (
                1
                -
                metrics.latency
            )
            * 0.20

            +

            (
                1
                -
                metrics.cost
            )
            * 0.15
        )

Bottleneck detector:

# bottlenecks.py


class Bottleneck:

    def detect(

        self,

        metrics,

    ):

        issues = []

        if metrics.latency > 0.70:

            issues.append(
                "slow"
            )

        if metrics.consistency < 0.60:

            issues.append(
                "unstable"
            )

        return issues

Weight tuner:

# tuning.py


class Tuner:

    def rebalance(

        self,

        weights,

        performance,

    ):

        updated = {}

        for k in weights:

            updated[k] = (

                weights[k]

                *

                (
                    1
                    +
                    performance[k]
                    * 0.03
                )

            )

        return updated

Safety validator:

# validator.py


class Validator:

    def approve(

        self,

        old_score,

        new_score,

    ):

        return (

            new_score
            >=

            old_score
        )

Change approval:

# approvals.py


class Approval:

    def allow(

        self,

        delta,

    ):

        return (

            delta
            <
            0.10
        )

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass
class Runtime:

Module registry:

# registry.py


class Registry:

    def __init__(self):

        self.modules = {}

    def register(

        self,

        name,

        instance,

    ):

        self.modules[
            name
        ] = instance

    def active(

        self,

    ):

        return list(
            self.modules
        )

Lifecycle manager:

# lifecycle.py


class Lifecycle:

    def start(

        self,

        modules,

    ):

        for m in modules:

            m.initialize()

    def stop(

        self,

        modules,

    ):

        for m in modules:

            m.shutdown()

Scheduler:

# scheduler.py


class Scheduler:

    def cycle(

        self,

        tasks,

    ):

        for t in tasks:

            t.execute()

Health monitor:

# health.py


class Health:

    def evaluate(

        self,

        metrics,

    ):

        return sum(
            metrics
        ) / len(
            metrics
        )

Checkpointing:

# checkpoint.py


class Checkpoint:

    def save(

        self,

        state,

    ):

        return state

    def restore(

        self,

        snapshot,

    ):

        return snapshot

Recovery:

# recovery.py


class Recovery:

    def recover(

        self,

        failed,

    ):

        return failed.restart()

@dataclass

Uncertainty scoring:

# meta_policy/uncertainty.py


class Uncertainty:

    def score(
        self,
        forecast,
    ):

        return (
            forecast.uncertainty
            +
            (
                1
                -
                max(
                    forecast.up,
                    forecast.down,
                    forecast.neutral,
                )
            )
        )

Conflict detector:

# meta_policy/conflict.py


class ConflictDetector:

    def detect(
        self,
        patterns,
    ):

        up = 0
        down = 0

        for p in patterns:

            if p.direction == "up":
                up += 1

            if p.direction == "down":
                down += 1

        return (
            up > 0
            and
            down > 0
        )

Risk evaluator:

# meta_policy/risk.py


class RiskEngine:

    def score(
        self,
        volatility,
        confidence,
    ):

        return (
            volatility
            *
            (
                1
                -
                confidence
            )
        )

Policy engine:

# meta_policy/policy.py


class MetaPolicy:

    def decide(
        self,
        forecast,
        memory,
        risk,
        conflict,
        uncertainty,
    ):

        if uncertainty > 0.55:

            return (
                "REQUEST_MORE_DATA"
            )

        if risk > 0.45:

            return (
                "CAUTION"
            )

        if conflict:

            return (
                "ABSTAIN"
            )

        return (
            "ACCEPT"
        )

Coordinator:

decision = policy.decide(

    forecast=forecast,

    memory=history_signal,

    risk=risk_score,

    conflict=has_conflict,

    uncertainty=uncertainty,
)

Outcome evaluator:

# learning/evaluator.py


class Evaluator:

    def evaluate(
        self,
        predicted,
        actual,
    ):

        error = abs(
            predicted
            -
            actual
        )

        accuracy = (
            1
            -
            error
        )

        return {

            "error":
                error,

            "accuracy":
                max(
                    0,
                    accuracy,
                ),
        }

Reward engine:

# learning/reward.py


class RewardEngine:

    def compute(
        self,
        accuracy,
        confidence,
    ):

        return (

            accuracy

            *

            (
                confidence
                + 0.25
            )

        )

Replay memory:

# learning/replay.py


class ReplayBuffer:

    def __init__(self):

        self.buffer = []

    def add(
        self,
        event,
    ):

        self.buffer.append(
            event
        )

    def sample(
        self,
        size=128,
    ):

        return self.buffer[
            -size:
        ]

Weight adaptation:

# learning/updater.py


class AdaptiveUpdater:

    def update(
        self,
        weights,
        reward,
    ):

        adjusted = {}

        for k, v in weights.items():

            adjusted[k] = (

                v
                *

                (
                    1
                    +
                    (
                        reward
                        -
                        0.5
                    )
                    *
                    0.05
                )

            )

        return adjusted

Drift detection:

# learning/drift.py


class DriftMonitor:

    def detect(
        self,
        recent_accuracy,
    ):

        return (
            recent_accuracy
            < 0.45
        )

Coordinator:

evaluation = evaluator.evaluate(

    predicted=
        forecast.expected_move,

    actual=
        observed_move,
)

reward = rewards.compute(

    evaluation["accuracy"],

    confidence,
)

weights = updater.update(

    weights,

    reward,
)

buffer.add(
    evaluation
)

  "drift_detected":false
}

# ingestion/types.py

@dataclass

Validator:

# ingestion/validator.py


class DataValidator:

    def validate(
        self,
        candles,
    ):

        clean = []

        for c in candles:

            if (
                c.low
                <= c.high
                and
                c.volume
                >= 0
            ):

                clean.append(
                    c
                )

        return clean

Outcome recorder:

# learning/real_outcomes.py


class OutcomeRecorder:

    def observe(
        self,
        prediction,
        future_close,
    ):

        move = (

            (
                future_close
                -
                prediction
            )

            /

            prediction

        )

        return move

Prediction journal:

# storage.py


class PredictionJournal:

    def __init__(self):

        self.records = []

    def save(
        self,
        record,
    ):

        self.records.append(
            record
        )

    def pending(
        self,
    ):

        return [

            r

            for r

            in self.records

            if not r["resolved"]

        ]

Resolution loop:

for p in journal.pending():

    outcome = recorder.observe(

        p["predicted_price"],

        current_close,
    )

    learner.update(

        prediction=
            p,

        outcome=
            outcome,
    )

    p["resolved"] = True

Think of learning as 4 separate streams:

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  GENERIC KNOWLEDGE
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

(static understanding)
        │
        ▼

Knowledge Layer
        │

────────────────────

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  REAL-TIME DATA
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

(current observations)
        │
        ▼

Observation Layer
        │

────────────────────

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  USER / ENVIRONMENT
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

(interactions)
        │
        ▼

Experience Layer
        │

────────────────────

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  OUTCOME FEEDBACK
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

(results)
        │
        ▼

Learning Layer

Then merge:

Knowledge
   +
Observation
   +
Experience
   +
Outcome
        ↓
Belief Engine
        ↓
Memory
        ↓
Decision

Recommended memory separation:

L0 → Rules / Constraints
L1 → Working State
L2 → Recent Observations
L3 → Generic Knowledge
L4 → Learned Experience

Example:

Generic knowledge says:

"High volatility often increases uncertainty"

Real-time data says:

BTC volatility = 8.2%

Experience says:

Past similar states failed prediction

Outcome says:

Recent confidence was overestimated

AARIYA updates:

Current belief:
Predict weaker.
Lower confidence.
Observe more.

Important distinction:

Do not let real-time overwrite generic knowledge directly.

Bad:

Real-time →
overwrite memory

Good:

Real-time
↓
temporary belief
↓
validate
↓
promote to memory

Use a learning gate:

class LearningGate:

    def accept(
        self,
        confidence,
        repetitions,
        evidence,
    ):

        return (

            confidence > 0.85

            and

            repetitions >= 5

            and

            evidence >= 3

        )

Meaning:

one event → observation only
repeated events → candidate memory
validated outcomes → learned knowledge

Result:

AARIYA becomes:

Knowledgeable
+
Adaptive
+
Evidence-driven
+
Real-time aware

instead of:

trained once
+
frozen forever


Meaning:

Generic Knowledge = foundation
Real-Time Data = authority
Experience = correction

Decision order:

REAL-TIME OBSERVATION     (highest)
↓
Validated EXPERIENCE
↓
Historical MEMORY
↓
GENERIC KNOWLEDGE         (lowest)

Architecture:

Realtime Stream
      │
      ▼
Observation Buffer

      │
      ▼

Priority Engine

 ┌──────────────┐
 │ Live Weight  │ 0.60
 │ Experience   │ 0.25
 │ History      │ 0.10
 │ Generic      │ 0.05
 └──────────────┘

      │
      ▼

Belief Engine
      │
      ▼
Decision

Priority controller:

# learning/priority.py


class PriorityEngine:

    def weights(
        self,
        freshness,
        confidence,
    ):

        if freshness > 0.90:

            return {

                "live": 0.70,

                "experience": 0.20,

                "memory": 0.07,

                "generic": 0.03,
            }

        return {

            "live": 0.45,

            "experience": 0.25,

            "memory": 0.20,

            "generic": 0.10,
        }

Belief merger:

# learning/belief.py


class BeliefEngine:

    def merge(

        self,

        live,

        experience,

        memory,

        generic,

        weights,

    ):

        return (

            live
            * weights["live"]

            +

            experience
            * weights["experience"]

            +

            memory
            * weights["memory"]

            +

            generic
            * weights["generic"]

        )

Continuous learning:

if realtime_confidence > 0.95:

    update_memory()

if repeated_events > 20:

    update_knowledge()

if outcomes_fail:

    reduce_weight()

Add decay so old knowledge fades:

# learning/decay.py

import math


def decay(

    age_hours,

):

    return math.exp(
        -age_hours
        / 48
    )

Normalizer:

# normalization.py


class Normalize:

    def convert(
        self,
        states,
    ):

        output = []

        for s in states:

            output.append({

                "symbol":
                    s.symbol,

                "return":
                    s.price,

                "risk":
                    s.volatility,
            })

        return output

# correlation.py

import numpy as np


class Correlation:

    def matrix(
        self,
        data,
    ):

        return np.corrcoef(
            data
        )

            "risk_on"
        )

Unified forecast:

# forecast.py


class UnifiedForecast:

    def combine(

        self,

        predictions,

    ):

        score = sum(
            predictions
        ) / len(
            predictions
        )

        return {

            "market_score":
                score
        }

Signal generator:

# generator.py


class Generator:

    def propose(

        self,

        features,

    ):

        proposals = []

        for f in features:

            proposals.append({

                "feature": f,

                "weight": 0.5,
            })

        return proposals

Performance tracker:

# tracker.py


class Tracker:

    def evaluate(

        self,

        predictions,

        outcomes,

    ):

        return sum(

            abs(
                p-o
            )

            for p, o

            in zip(
                predictions,
                outcomes,
            )

        )

Alpha score:

# scoring.py


class Score:

    def compute(

        self,

        hit_rate,

        stability,

        drawdown,

    ):

        return (

            hit_rate
            * 0.5

            +

            stability
            * 0.3

            +

            (
                1
                -
                drawdown
            )
            * 0.2
        )

Validation:

# validation.py


class Validate:

    def approve(

        self,

        observations,

        score,

    ):

        return (

            observations
            > 100

            and

            score
            > 0.70
        )

Retirement:

# retirement.py


class Retire:

    def remove(

        self,

        score,

    ):

        return (

            score
            < 0.45
        )

Registry:

# registry.py


class Registry:

    def __init__(self):

        self.alphas = []

    def register(

        self,

        alpha,

    ):

        self.alphas.append(
            alpha
        )

candidate = generate()

if approve():

    register()

    retire()

        self,

        growth,

        margins,

        leverage,

                -
                leverage
            )
            * 0.2
        )

        )

Attribution engine:

# attribution.py


class Attribution:

    def explain(

        self,

        forecast,

        outcome,

    ):

        return {

            "error":

            abs(
                forecast
                -
                outcome
            )
        }

        agreement,

            * 0.6

            agreement
            * 0.4
        )

Allocation engine:

# capital/deployment.py


class Allocation:

    def deploy(

        self,

        conviction,

        uncertainty,

    ):

        usable = (

            conviction

            *

            (
                1
                -
                uncertainty
            )

        )

        return {

            "deploy":
                usable
        }

Risk budgeting:

# capital/budgeting.py


class Budget:

    def assign(

        self,

        signals,

    ):

        total = sum(
            signals
        )

        return [

            s
            /
            total

            for s

            in signals
        ]

Exposure controller:

# capital/exposure.py


class Exposure:

    def limit(

        self,

        exposure,

    ):

        return min(
            exposure,
            0.80,
        )

Rebalancer:

# capital/rebalance.py


class Rebalance:

    def adjust(

        self,

        current,

        target,

    ):

        return {

            k:

            target[k]

            -

            current.get(
                k,
                0,
            )

            for k

            in target
        }

        self.nodes.append(
            node
        )

    def connect(

        self,

        a,

        b,

    ):

        self.edges.append(
            (
                a,
                b,
            )
        )

Replay engine:

# player.py


class ReplayEngine:

    def run(

        self,

        events,

    ):

        for e in events:

            yield e

# decisions.py


class DecisionScore:

        self,

        actual,

    ):

            predicted
            ==
            actual
        )

Outcome tracker:

# outcomes.py


class Outcome:

    def result(

        self,

        forecast,

        market,

    ):

        return {

            "error":

            abs(

                forecast
                -
                market

            )
        }

Stress engine:

# drawdown.py


class Stress:

    def evaluate(

        self,

        peak,

        current,

    ):

        return (

            peak
            -
            current

        )

        / peak

# metrics.py


class Metrics:

    def score(

        self,

        accuracy,

        drawdown,

        stability,

    ):

        return (

            accuracy
            * 0.5

            +

            (
                1
                -
                drawdown
            )
            * 0.3

            +

            stability
            * 0.2
        )

Comparison:

# compare.py


class Compare:

    def winner(

        self,

        baseline,

        candidate,

    ):

        if candidate > baseline:

            return "candidate"

        return "baseline"

class Rollout:

        self,

        percentage,

    ):

        return min(

            percentage
            +
            10,

            100,
        )

# builder.py

        self,

        config,

    ):

        return StrategyGenome(

            id="v1",

            allocation_weight=0.10,

            confidence_weight=0.10,
        )

Mutation:

# mutation.py


class Mutation:

    def apply(

        self,

        genome,

        scale=0.03,

    ):

        genome.forecast_weight += scale

        genome.risk_weight -= scale

        return genome

Fitness:

# fitness.py


class Fitness:

    def evaluate(

        self,

        accuracy,

        drawdown,

        stability,

        adaptability,

    ):

        return (

            accuracy
            * 0.35

            +

            (
                1
                -
                drawdown
            )
            * 0.30

            +

            stability
            * 0.20

            +

            adaptability
            * 0.15
        )

Tournament:

# tournament.py


class Tournament:

    def select(

        self,

        genomes,

    ):

        return max(

            genomes,

            key=lambda g:

            g.score
        )

class Promotion:

    def allow(

        self,

        challenger,

        incumbent,

    ):

        return (

            challenger
            >
            incumbent
            *
            1.03
        )

        self,

# sectors.py


class Sector:

        self,

        sectors,

    ):

        return sorted(

            sectors,

            reverse=True,
        )

        self,

        volume,

        spread,

    ):

        return (

            volume

            /

                +
                1
            )
        )

Economic regimes:

# regimes.py


class Regime:

    def detect(

        self,

        inflation,

        growth,

        liquidity,

    ):

        if growth > 0.7:

            return "expansion"

        if liquidity < 0.3:

            return "tight"

        return "neutral"

# lessons.py


class Extract:

        self,

        outcomes,

                "quality"

            > 0.8

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  3. Self Evaluation Engine
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Problem

No automatic improvement loop.

Solution
class Evaluator:

    def evaluate(
        self,
        prediction,
        actual
    ):
        error = abs(prediction - actual)
        return error
class LearningLoop:

    def __init__(self, learning_rate=0.01):
        self.learning_rate = learning_rate
        self.weights = {}
        self.error_history = []

    def update(self, error):
        self.error_history.append(error)
        for key in self.weights:
            self.weights[key] *= (1 - self.learning_rate * error)
        if len(self.error_history) > 100:
            self.error_history.pop(0)

    def convergence_rate(self):
        if len(self.error_history) < 10:
            return 1.0
        recent = self.error_history[-10:]
        return 1 - (sum(recent) / len(recent))

Pipeline:

Prediction
    ↓
Actual Result
    ↓
Error Score
    ↓
Learning Update

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  10. Simulation Engine
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Run multiple futures.

class Simulator:

    def __init__(self, num_branches=5, noise_std=0.1):
        self.num_branches = num_branches
        self.noise_std = noise_std

    def run(
        self,
        scenario
    ):
        branches = []
        base = scenario.copy() if isinstance(scenario, dict) else {"input": scenario}
        for i in range(self.num_branches):
            branch = base.copy()
            branch["branch_id"] = i
            branch["probability"] = 1.0 / self.num_branches
            for key, val in branch.items():
                if isinstance(val, (int, float)):
                    noise = random.gauss(0, self.noise_std * abs(val) if val else self.noise_std)
                    branch[key] = val + noise
            branches.append(branch)
        return branches

class SkillFactory:

    def __init__(self):
        self.catalog = {}

    def create_skill(
        self,
        successful_workflows
    ):
        signature = tuple(
            tuple(w.items()) if isinstance(w, dict) else (w,)
            for w in successful_workflows[-3:]
        )
        skill_id = f"skill_{hash(signature)}"
        if skill_id not in self.catalog:
            skill = {
                "id": skill_id,
                "workflows": successful_workflows,
                "patterns": self.extract_patterns(successful_workflows),
                "confidence": 0.5,
                "usage_count": 0
            }
            self.catalog[skill_id] = skill
        return self.catalog[skill_id]

    def extract_patterns(self, workflows):
        common_steps = {}
        for wf in workflows:
            for step in (wf if isinstance(wf, list) else [wf]):
                key = str(step)[:50]
                common_steps[key] = common_steps.get(key, 0) + 1
        threshold = len(workflows) * 0.5
        return [k for k, v in common_steps.items() if v >= threshold]

AARIYA begins generating new skills from experience.

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Phase 18 — Continuous Architecture Optimizer
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

AARIYA should analyze itself.

class ArchitectureOptimizer:

    def __init__(self):
        self.metrics = {}
        self.latency_threshold = 1.0
        self.suggestion_rules = {
            "memory": "Add vector cache layer",
            "retrieval": "Index with HNSW or FAISS",
            "prediction": "Reduce ensemble size or distill",
            "planning": "Cache plan tree with LRU"
        }

    def detect_bottlenecks(self):
        bottlenecks = []
        for component, stats in self.metrics.items():
            score = 0
            if stats.get("latency", 0) > self.latency_threshold:
                score += 1
            if stats.get("error_rate", 0) > 0.1:
                score += 2
            if stats.get("memory_mb", 0) > 512:
                score += 1
            if score >= 2:
                bottlenecks.append({"component": component, "severity": score, "stats": stats})
        return sorted(bottlenecks, key=lambda b: b["severity"], reverse=True)

    def suggest_improvements(self):
        improvements = []
        for b in self.detect_bottlenecks():
            rule_key = next(
                (k for k in self.suggestion_rules if k in b["component"].lower()),
                "generic"
            )
            suggestion = self.suggestion_rules.get(
                rule_key,
                "Profile and optimize hot path"
            )
            improvements.append({
                "component": b["component"],
                "suggestion": suggestion,
                "expected_gain": b["severity"] * 0.1
            })
        return improvements

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Layer 7 — Unified Agent Interface
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Every agent:

class Agent:

    def __init__(self):
        self.skills = []
        self.capability_profile = {}

    def register_skill(self, skill):
        self.skills.append(skill)

    def can_handle(self, task):
        task_type = self.classify(task)
        for skill in self.skills:
            if skill.get("type") == task_type or skill.get("match", lambda _: False)(task):
                return True
        return task_type in self.capability_profile

    def classify(self, task):
        if isinstance(task, dict):
            return task.get("type", "unknown")
        return type(task).__name__

    def execute(self, task):
        task_type = self.classify(task)
        for skill in self.skills:
            if skill.get("type") == task_type:
                return skill.get("handler", lambda t: t)(task)
        return {"status": "unhandled", "task_type": task_type}

    def evaluate(self, result):
        if result is None:
            return {"score": 0.0, "passed": False, "reason": "no_result"}
        if isinstance(result, dict):
            quality = result.get("quality", 0.5)
            completeness = result.get("completeness", 0.5)
        else:
            quality = 0.5
            completeness = 0.5
        score = quality * completeness
        return {"score": score, "passed": score > 0.6, "quality": quality, "completeness": completeness}

Then adding future agents becomes trivial.

  2. Confidence Calibration
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

BTC will rise.

BTC may rise.

Confidence: 62%

Over time learn:

60% confidence → actually correct 60%?
80% confidence → actually correct 80%?

This is a huge difference between amateur and professional forecasting systems.

# core/contracts.py
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Dict, Any, List, Union

@dataclass
class PredictionRequest:
    domain: str                 # weather | stocks | crypto
    entity: str                # e.g. AAPL, BTC, Colombo
    horizon: int               # time horizon
    features: Dict[str, Any]
    context: Dict[str, Any]

@dataclass
class PredictionOutput:
    value: Union[float, Dict[str, Any]]
    confidence: float
    uncertainty: float
    reasoning_trace: List[str]
    model_version: str

@dataclass
class StandardDataPoint:
    timestamp: float
    entity: str
    features: Dict[str, Any]
    source: str
    reliability_score: float

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  2. DATA ABSTRACTION LAYER
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

# core/data_layer.py
class DataNormalizer:
    def normalize(self, raw_data: dict, source: str) -> StandardDataPoint:
        return StandardDataPoint(
            timestamp=raw_data["timestamp"],
            entity=raw_data["entity"],
            features=raw_data["features"],
            source=source,
            reliability_score=self._score(source)
        )

    def _score(self, source: str) -> float:
        scores = {
            "weather_api": 0.9,
            "stock_api": 0.95,
            "crypto_api": 0.92
        }
        return scores.get(source, 0.5)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  5. PREDICTION VERIFICATION ENGINE
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

# engines/verification_engine.py
class VerificationEngine:

    def __init__(self):
        self.history = []

    def log_prediction(self, pred, actual):
        error = abs(pred.value - actual)

        record = {
            "prediction": pred.value,
            "actual": actual,
            "error": error,
            "confidence": pred.confidence
        }

        self.history.append(record)

    def accuracy(self):
        if not self.history:
            return 0.0
        return sum(1 for h in self.history if h["error"] < 5) / len(self.history)

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  6. ORCHESTRATION ENGINE (BRAIN LOOP)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

# orchestrator/main_loop.py
from engines.prediction_core import PredictionCoreEngine
from engines.verification_engine import VerificationEngine

class AariyaOrchestrator:

    def __init__(self):
        self.engine = PredictionCoreEngine()
        self.verifier = VerificationEngine()

    def run_cycle(self, request):

        prediction = self.engine.predict(request)

        print("PREDICTION:", prediction)

        return prediction

    def feedback(self, prediction, actual_value):
        self.verifier.log_prediction(prediction, actual_value)

        print("CURRENT ACCURACY:", self.verifier.accuracy())

🧠 2. CAUSAL GRAPH ENGINE (CORE CODE)

This is what turns AARIYA from:

“predictor” → “reasoning system”

📌 causal_graph_engine.py
# engines/causal/graph_engine.py

from collections import defaultdict
import math

class CausalGraphEngine:

    def __init__(self):
        # adjacency list: node -> [(target, weight, lag)]
        self.graph = defaultdict(list)

    def add_edge(self, cause, effect, weight=0.5, lag=1):
        """
        weight: strength of causal influence (0–1)
        lag: time delay impact
        """
        self.graph[cause].append({
            "effect": effect,
            "weight": weight,
            "lag": lag
        })

    def infer_effect(self, cause_signal: dict):
        """
        propagate causal influence forward
        """
        results = defaultdict(float)

        for cause, value in cause_signal.items():
            if cause not in self.graph:
                continue

            for edge in self.graph[cause]:
                impact = value * edge["weight"]

                # decay with lag
                impact *= math.exp(-0.1 * edge["lag"])

                results[edge["effect"]] += impact

        return dict(results)

    def explain(self, target):
        """
        reverse traversal (why prediction happened)
        """
        explanations = []

        for cause, edges in self.graph.items():
            for e in edges:
                if e["effect"] == target:
                    explanations.append({
                        "cause": cause,
                        "weight": e["weight"],
                        "lag": e["lag"]
                    })

        return explanations
🧠 3. ML-BASED PREDICTION ENGINE (XGBOOST VERSION)
# engines/prediction/xgboost_engine.py

import xgboost as xgb
import numpy as np

class XGBoostPredictionEngine:

    def __init__(self):
        self.model = xgb.XGBRegressor(
            n_estimators=200,
            max_depth=6,
            learning_rate=0.05
        )

        self.trained = False

    def train(self, X, y):
        self.model.fit(X, y)
        self.trained = True

    def predict(self, X):
        if not self.trained:
            raise Exception("Model not trained")

        preds = self.model.predict(X)
        confidence = np.clip(np.std(preds) / (np.mean(preds)+1e-6), 0, 1)

        return {
            "prediction": float(preds[0]),
            "confidence": float(1 - confidence)
        }
🧠 4. SIGNAL FUSION LAYER (IMPORTANT)

Combines multiple data sources intelligently.

# engines/fusion/signal_fusion.py

class SignalFusionEngine:

    def fuse(self, signals: list):
        """
        signals = [
            {"value": 10, "weight": 0.8},
            {"value": 12, "weight": 0.6}
        ]
        """

        numerator = 0
        denominator = 0

        for s in signals:
            numerator += s["value"] * s["weight"]
            denominator += s["weight"]

        return numerator / (denominator + 1e-6)
🧠 5. DRIFT DETECTION ENGINE

Detects when model becomes wrong over time.

# engines/verification/drift_detector.py

import numpy as np

class DriftDetector:

    def __init__(self):
        self.errors = []

    def log_error(self, predicted, actual):
        self.errors.append(abs(predicted - actual))

    def drift_score(self):
        if len(self.errors) < 10:
            return 0.0

        recent = np.mean(self.errors[-10:])
        past = np.mean(self.errors[:-10])

        return abs(recent - past)
🧠 6. OPPORTUNITY ENGINE (WHAT → ACTION)
# engines/decision/opportunity_engine.py

class OpportunityEngine:

    def generate(self, prediction):
        value = prediction["prediction"]

        if value > 0.7:
            return "BUY / POSITIVE ACTION"
        elif value < -0.7:
            return "SELL / NEGATIVE ACTION"
        else:
            return "HOLD / OBSERVE"

🧠 1. WHAT CHANGES WHEN YOU “SHIFT TO ML”
BEFORE (your current system)
Features → Rules / Hash / Weighted logic → Output
Problem:
no learning
no adaptation
no real probability model
AFTER (ML system)
Historical Data → Feature Store → Model Training → Prediction → Backtesting → Retraining
🧠 2. FULL ML TRANSITION ARCHITECTURE
CORE SHIFT:
Layer	Old System	New System
Prediction	rules/hash	XGBoost / Transformer
Learning	none	continuous training
Confidence	fake	calibrated probability
Memory	stateless	historical dataset
Improvement	manual	automatic retraining
🧠 3. STEP 1 — DATASET CREATION PIPELINE (CRITICAL)

You CANNOT do ML without this.

# data/training_dataset.py

import pandas as pd

class DatasetBuilder:

    def build(self, raw_data):

        dataset = []

        for d in raw_data:
            row = {
                "feature_1": d["price"],
                "feature_2": d["volume"],
                "feature_3": d["volatility"],
                "target": d["future_return"]  # IMPORTANT: label
            }
            dataset.append(row)

        return pd.DataFrame(dataset)
🧠 4. STEP 2 — XGBOOST CORE MODEL (REAL ML REPLACEMENT)

This replaces your rule-based engine.

# engines/ml/xgboost_model.py

import xgboost as xgb
import numpy as np

class AariyaXGBoostModel:

    def __init__(self):

        self.model = xgb.XGBRegressor(
            n_estimators=500,
            max_depth=8,
            learning_rate=0.03,
            subsample=0.8,
            colsample_bytree=0.8
        )

        self.is_trained = False

    def train(self, X, y):
        self.model.fit(X, y)
        self.is_trained = True

    def predict(self, X):

        if not self.is_trained:
            raise Exception("Model not trained")

        prediction = self.model.predict(X)[0]

        return {
            "prediction": float(prediction),
            "confidence": self._confidence(X),
        }

    def _confidence(self, X):
        # proxy confidence (later replace with calibration model)
        variance = np.std(self.model.predict(X.reshape(1, -1)))
        return float(max(0.1, 1 - variance))
🧠 5. STEP 3 — TRANSFORMER TIME SERIES MODEL (ADVANCED)

For stock/crypto/weather sequences.

# engines/ml/transformer_model.py

import torch
import torch.nn as nn

class SimpleTransformer(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, input_dim, d_model=64):
        super().__init__()

        self.embedding = nn.Linear(input_dim, d_model)

        self.transformer = nn.TransformerEncoder(
            nn.TransformerEncoderLayer(d_model=d_model, nhead=4),
            num_layers=2
        )

        self.output = nn.Linear(d_model, 1)

    def forward(self, x):

        x = self.embedding(x)
        x = self.transformer(x)
        x = x.mean(dim=1)
        return self.output(x)
🧠 6. STEP 4 — ML-BASED PREDICTION ENGINE (REPLACES RULE ENGINE)
# engines/prediction/ml_engine.py

class MLPredictionEngine:

    def __init__(self, model):
        self.model = model

    def predict(self, features):

        result = self.model.predict(features)

        return {
            "value": result["prediction"],
            "confidence": result["confidence"],
            "type": "ml_based"
        }
🧠 7. STEP 5 — BACKTESTING ENGINE (CRITICAL FOR REAL SYSTEMS)
# engines/verification/backtester.py

class Backtester:

    def run(self, model, dataset):

        errors = []

        for row in dataset:
            pred = model.predict(row["features"])["prediction"]
            actual = row["target"]

            errors.append(abs(pred - actual))

        return {
            "mae": sum(errors) / len(errors),
            "accuracy_score": self._accuracy(errors)
        }

    def _accuracy(self, errors):
        return sum(1 for e in errors if e < 0.05) / len(errors)

📁 data/ingestion/live_feeds.py
import requests
import pandas as pd

class LiveDataFeed:

    # ---------------- STOCKS ----------------
    def fetch_stock(self, symbol: str):
        url = f"https://query1.finance.yahoo.com/v7/finance/quote?symbols={symbol}"
        data = requests.get(url).json()

        result = data["quoteResponse"]["result"][0]

        return {
            "price": result["regularMarketPrice"],
            "volume": result["regularMarketVolume"],
            "change": result["regularMarketChangePercent"]
        }

    # ---------------- CRYPTO ----------------
    def fetch_crypto(self, symbol="BTCUSDT"):
        url = f"https://api.binance.com/api/v3/ticker/24hr?symbol={symbol}"
        data = requests.get(url).json()

        return {
            "price": float(data["lastPrice"]),
            "volume": float(data["volume"]),
            "change": float(data["priceChangePercent"])
        }

    # ---------------- WEATHER ----------------
    def fetch_weather(self, city: str, api_key: str):
        url = f"https://api.openweathermap.org/data/2.5/weather?q={city}&appid={api_key}"
        data = requests.get(url).json()

        return {
            "temp": data["main"]["temp"],
            "humidity": data["main"]["humidity"],
            "pressure": data["main"]["pressure"]
        }
🔥 2. FULL TRAINING PIPELINE AUTOMATION
🎯 Purpose:

Turn ML from manual → continuous learning system

📁 learning/training_pipeline.py
import schedule
import time

class TrainingPipeline:

    def __init__(self, model, dataset_builder, trainer):
        self.model = model
        self.dataset_builder = dataset_builder
        self.trainer = trainer

    def run_training(self, raw_data):

        dataset = self.dataset_builder.build(raw_data)

        X = dataset.drop("target", axis=1).values
        y = dataset["target"].values

        self.model.train(X, y)

        print("✅ Model retrained successfully")

    def schedule_training(self, raw_data):

        # retrain every 6 hours
        schedule.every(6).hours.do(
            lambda: self.run_training(raw_data)
        )

        while True:
            schedule.run_pending()
            time.sleep(60)
🔥 3. DRIFT DETECTION SYSTEM (MODEL DEGRADATION CONTROL)
🎯 Purpose:

Detect when ML model becomes unreliable

📁 engines/verification/drift_detector.py
import numpy as np

class DriftDetector:

    def __init__(self):
        self.recent_errors = []
        self.baseline_errors = []

    def log(self, predicted, actual):

        error = abs(predicted - actual)

        self.recent_errors.append(error)

        if len(self.recent_errors) > 50:
            self.baseline_errors.append(self.recent_errors.pop(0))

    def detect_drift(self):

        if len(self.baseline_errors) < 20:
            return 0.0

        recent_mean = np.mean(self.recent_errors)
        baseline_mean = np.mean(self.baseline_errors)

        drift = abs(recent_mean - baseline_mean)

        return drift

    def is_model_degrading(self, threshold=0.15):
        return self.detect_drift() > threshold
🔥 4. CAUSAL GRAPH + ML INTEGRATION (FINAL EVOLUTION STEP)
🎯 Purpose:

Not just predict → understand WHY prediction happens

📁 engines/causal/ml_causal_bridge.py
class ML_Causal_Bridge:

    def __init__(self, causal_engine):
        self.causal_engine = causal_engine

    def enhance_features(self, features):

        # inject causal signals into ML input space
        causal_signals = self.causal_engine.infer_effect(features)

        merged = {**features}

        for k, v in causal_signals.items():
            merged[f"causal_{k}"] = v

        return merged

    def explain_prediction(self, target):

        return self.causal_engine.explain(target)

import mlflow

def log_experiment(params, metrics):

    mlflow.start_run()

    for k, v in params.items():
        mlflow.log_param(k, v)

    for k, v in metrics.items():
        mlflow.log_metric(k, v)

    mlflow.end_run()
🚀 2. TRANSFORMER FORECASTING ENGINE (CORE INTELLIGENCE)
🎯 Purpose:

Replace simple ML → deep temporal reasoning

📁 MODEL
import torch
import torch.nn as nn

class TimeSeriesTransformer(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, input_dim=10, d_model=128):
        super().__init__()

        self.embedding = nn.Linear(input_dim, d_model)

        self.encoder = nn.TransformerEncoder(
            nn.TransformerEncoderLayer(d_model=d_model, nhead=8),
            num_layers=4
        )

        self.head = nn.Linear(d_model, 1)

    def forward(self, x):

        x = self.embedding(x)
        x = self.encoder(x)
        x = x.mean(dim=1)

        return self.head(x)
🧠 Why this matters

This model learns:

market cycles
volatility structure
weather temporal shifts
cross-time dependencies
🚀 3. FULL BACKTESTING + EVALUATION SYSTEM
🎯 Purpose:

Make predictions scientifically valid

📁 BACKTEST ENGINE
class BacktestEngine:

    def run(self, model, dataset):

        results = []

        for sample in dataset:

            pred = model.predict(sample["x"])
            actual = sample["y"]

            results.append({
                "pred": pred,
                "actual": actual,
                "error": abs(pred - actual)
            })

        return self.metrics(results)

    def metrics(self, results):

        errors = [r["error"] for r in results]

        return {
            "MAE": sum(errors) / len(errors),
            "Accuracy": sum(1 for e in errors if e < 0.05) / len(errors)
        }
📊 WHY THIS IS CRITICAL

Without backtesting:

ML system is blind guessing

With backtesting:

You have measurable intelligence

class DecisionEngine:

    def decide(self, prediction):

        p = prediction["value"]
        c = prediction["confidence"]

        if c < 0.5:
            return "WAIT"

        if p > 0.7:
            return "BUY / POSITIVE ACTION"

        if p < -0.7:
            return "SELL / NEGATIVE ACTION"

        return "MONITOR"

🧠 1. Reinforcement Learning Decision Engine (RL Layer)
What it is

A system that learns:

“Which action produces the best long-term reward?”

Core idea

Instead of:

predicting value → then deciding manually

It learns:

State → Action → Reward → Policy Update
Use in AARIYA
trading decisions
buy/sell timing
resource allocation
API usage optimization
Minimal RL structure
class RLAgent:

    def __init__(self):
        self.q_table = {}

    def choose_action(self, state):
        return max(self.q_table.get(state, {}), key=lambda a: a[1], default="hold")

    def update(self, state, action, reward):
        self.q_table.setdefault(state, {})
        self.q_table[state][action] = reward
Limitation
needs massive data
unstable early-stage
hard to debug

Architecture
class Agent:
    def __init__(self):
        self.memory = {}
    def act(self, data):
        if isinstance(data, dict):
            processed = {k: (v if isinstance(v, (int, float)) else hash(str(v)) % 100) for k, v in data.items()}
            return sum(processed.values()) / max(len(processed), 1)
        if isinstance(data, (list, tuple)):
            vals = [v for v in data if isinstance(v, (int, float))]
            return sum(vals) / max(len(vals), 1) if vals else 0.0
        return float(hash(str(data)) % 1000) / 1000

class ForecastAgent(Agent):
    def act(self, data):
        return {"forecast": sum(data)/len(data)}

class RiskAgent(Agent):
    def act(self, data):
        return {"risk": max(data)}

class Swarm:
    def __init__(self, agents):
        self.agents = agents

    def run(self, data):
        results = {}
        for a in self.agents:
            results.update(a.act(data))
        return results
Strength
scalable intelligence
modular reasoning
closest to “AI system design used in research labs”

from collections import defaultdict

class KnowledgeGraph:

    def __init__(self):
        self.graph = defaultdict(list)

    def add_relation(self, a, relation, b):
        self.graph[a].append((relation, b))

    def query(self, node):
        return self.graph.get(node, [])

🧠 2. KNOWLEDGE GRAPH MEMORY (FOUNDATION LAYER)
📁 memory/knowledge_graph.py
from collections import defaultdict

class KnowledgeGraph:

    def __init__(self):
        self.graph = defaultdict(list)
        self.events = []

    def add_relation(self, src, relation, dst, weight=1.0):

        self.graph[src].append({
            "relation": relation,
            "target": dst,
            "weight": weight
        })

    def add_event(self, event):

        self.events.append(event)

    def query(self, node):

        return self.graph.get(node, [])

    def context_vector(self, node):

        edges = self.graph.get(node, [])
        return sum(e["weight"] for e in edges)
🧠 3. MULTI-AGENT SWARM (STRUCTURE LAYER)

Each agent has a job.

📁 agents/base.py
class Agent:

    def __init__(self):
        self.state = {}

    def run(self, data, memory):
        context = memory.get(self.__class__.__name__, {})
        features = self.extract(data, context)
        prediction = self.predict(features)
        memory[self.__class__.__name__] = {"last_input": data, "last_output": prediction}
        return prediction

    def extract(self, data, context):
        merged = {}
        if isinstance(data, dict):
            merged.update(data)
        if isinstance(context, dict):
            merged.update(context)
        return merged

    def predict(self, features):
        vals = [v for v in features.values() if isinstance(v, (int, float))]
        return sum(vals) / max(len(vals), 1) if vals else 0.5
Forecast Agent
class ForecastAgent(Agent):

    def run(self, data, memory):

        return {
            "forecast": sum(data.values()) / len(data),
            "confidence": 0.7
        }
Risk Agent
class RiskAgent(Agent):

    def run(self, data, memory):

        risk = max(data.values()) / (sum(data.values()) + 1e-6)

        return {
            "risk": risk
        }
Swarm Controller
class Swarm:

    def __init__(self, agents):
        self.agents = agents

    def run(self, data, memory):

        outputs = {}

        for agent in self.agents:
            outputs.update(agent.run(data, memory))

        return outputs
🧠 4. RL DECISION ENGINE (ADAPTATION LAYER)

This is the learning brain.

📁 rl/agent.py
import random

class RLDecisionEngine:

    def __init__(self):
        self.q = {}

    def state_key(self, state):
        return str(state)

    def choose_action(self, state):

        key = self.state_key(state)

        if key not in self.q:
            return random.choice(["buy", "sell", "hold"])

        return max(self.q[key], key=self.q[key].get)

    def update(self, state, action, reward):

        key = self.state_key(state)

        if key not in self.q:
            self.q[key] = {}

        self.q[key][action] = self.q[key].get(action, 0) + reward

        return "NO ACTION"
🧠 6. FULL INTEGRATION BRAIN (AARIYA CORE LOOP)
📁 core/system.py
class AariyaSystem:

    def __init__(self, memory, swarm, rl, executor):
        self.memory = memory
        self.swarm = swarm
        self.rl = rl
        self.executor = executor

    def step(self, data):

        # 1. memory enrichment
        context = self.memory.context_vector("market")

        data["context"] = context

        # 2. swarm reasoning
        swarm_output = self.swarm.run(data, self.memory)

        # 3. RL decision
        state = swarm_output
        action = self.rl.choose_action(state)

        # 4. execution
        result = self.executor.execute(action, "BTC", 1)

        return {
            "swarm": swarm_output,
            "action": action,
            "execution": result
        }

🔥 1. REWARD FUNCTION DESIGN (CORE OF RL)

This is the MOST important part of autonomy.

📁 rl/reward_function.py
class RewardFunction:

    def compute(self, state, action, next_state):

        # profit / improvement signal
        profit = next_state["value"] - state["value"]

        # risk penalty
        risk_penalty = next_state.get("risk", 0) * 0.5

        # stability reward (avoid noise chasing)
        stability_bonus = 0.1 if abs(profit) < 0.05 else 0

        reward = profit - risk_penalty + stability_bonus

        return reward
🧠 Why this matters

Your AI now learns:

“profit is good”
“risk is bad”
“stable decisions are preferred”

👉 THIS defines intelligence behavior.

🔥 2. REAL DATASET TRAINING LOOP

This is how AARIYA becomes “experienced”.

📁 training/training_loop.py
class TrainingLoop:

    def __init__(self, model, dataset, reward_fn):
        self.model = model
        self.dataset = dataset
        self.reward_fn = reward_fn

    def train(self):

        for episode in self.dataset:

            state = episode["state"]

            action = self.model.act(state)

            next_state = episode["next_state"]

            reward = self.reward_fn.compute(state, action, next_state)

            self.model.learn(state, action, reward)
🧠 Key idea

Instead of labels:

You train using REWARD signals, not static answers.

🔥 3. SIMULATION ENVIRONMENT (PAPER TRADING)

This is where AARIYA learns safely.

📁 simulator/paper_trading_env.py
import random

class PaperTradingEnv:

    def __init__(self):
        self.price = 100

    def step(self, action):

        # simulate market movement
        self.price += random.uniform(-2, 2)

        reward = 0

        if action == "buy":
            reward = random.uniform(-1, 3)

        elif action == "sell":
            reward = random.uniform(-3, 2)

        else:
            reward = 0.1  # holding stability

        return {
            "value": self.price,
            "reward": reward
        }
🧠 Why this is critical

This allows:

safe experimentation
RL training without real money loss
policy stabilization
🔥 4. SAFETY CONSTRAINT LAYER (GUARDRAILS)

This prevents “AI collapse behavior”.

📁 safety/constraint_layer.py
class SafetyLayer:

    def __init__(self):
        self.max_risk = 0.3
        self.max_position = 10

    def validate(self, action, context):

        if context["risk"] > self.max_risk:
            return "BLOCKED: HIGH RISK"

        if context["position_size"] > self.max_position:
            return "BLOCKED: POSITION LIMIT"

        if action not in ["buy", "sell", "hold"]:
            return "BLOCKED: INVALID ACTION"

        return "APPROVED"
🧠 Why this matters

Without this layer:

RL systems can become unstable or destructive

With this:

AI becomes controllable and production-safe

🔥 FULL AUTONOMOUS LOOP (FINAL SYSTEM)
📁 core/autonomous_loop.py
class AutonomousAariya:

    def __init__(self, env, model, reward_fn, safety):
        self.env = env
        self.model = model
        self.reward_fn = reward_fn
        self.safety = safety

    def run(self):

        state = self.env.step("hold")

        for _ in range(1000):

            action = self.model.act(state)

            decision = self.safety.validate(action, {
                "risk": state.get("risk", 0),
                "position_size": 1
            })

            if "BLOCKED" in decision:
                action = "hold"

            next_state = self.env.step(action)

            reward = self.reward_fn.compute(state, action, next_state)

            self.model.learn(state, action, reward)

            state = next_state

Minimal code (real structure)
🧠 Base RL Agent
class RLAgent:

    def __init__(self, name):
        self.name = name
        self.q = {}

    def act(self, state):
        return self.q.get(str(state), "hold")

    def learn(self, state, action, reward):
        key = str(state)
        self.q.setdefault(key, {})
        self.q[key][action] = reward
🧠 Swarm Controller
class RLSwarm:

    def __init__(self, agents):
        self.agents = agents

    def act(self, state):

        votes = {}

        for agent in self.agents:
            action = agent.act(state)
            votes[action] = votes.get(action, 0) + 1

        return max(votes, key=votes.get)

    def learn(self, state, action, reward):

        for agent in self.agents:
            agent.learn(state, action, reward)
Why this is powerful

✔ specialization emerges
✔ reduces single-model failure
✔ better robustness
✔ scalable intelligence structure

Code
from collections import defaultdict

class MemoryGraph:

    def __init__(self):
        self.graph = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(float))

    def update(self, a, relation, b, strength=1.0):
        self.graph[a][(relation, b)] += strength

    def query(self, a):
        return self.graph.get(a, {})
Why it matters

✔ builds long-term intelligence
✔ improves RL state quality
✔ enables cross-domain reasoning

🧠 2. CORE DESIGN IDEA (IMPORTANT)

Instead of ONE reward function:

You split intelligence into:

🔥 3. CODE — BASE RL AGENT (REAL STRUCTURE)
class RLAgent:

    def __init__(self, name, reward_weight=1.0):
        self.name = name
        self.q_table = {}
        self.reward_weight = reward_weight

    def act(self, state):
        return self.q_table.get(str(state), "hold")

    def update(self, state, action, reward):

        key = str(state)

        if key not in self.q_table:
            self.q_table[key] = {}

        self.q_table[key][action] = self.q_table[key].get(action, 0) + reward * self.reward_weight
🧠 4. SPECIALIZED AGENTS
Trader Agent (profit maximizer)
class TraderAgent(RLAgent):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__(action_dim=5, reward_weight=1.0)
        self.profit_log = []

    def act(self, state):
        key = str(state)
        if key in self.q_table:
            return max(self.q_table[key], key=self.q_table[key].get)
        return "hold"

    def update(self, state, action, reward):
        super().update(state, action, reward * 1.2)

Risk Agent (penalizes instability)
class RiskAgent(RLAgent):

    def update(self, state, action, reward):
        # invert reward logic
        super().update(state, action, -abs(reward))
Signal Agent (trend follower)
class SignalAgent(RLAgent):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__(action_dim=3, reward_weight=0.8)
        self.trend_window = deque(maxlen=20)

    def act(self, state):
        if isinstance(state, (list, tuple)):
            self.trend_window.append(sum(state) / len(state))
        else:
            self.trend_window.append(float(state))
        if len(self.trend_window) >= 5:
            recent = list(self.trend_window)[-5:]
            slope = (recent[-1] - recent[0]) / len(recent)
            if slope > 0.01:
                return "up"
            elif slope < -0.01:
                return "down"
        return "neutral"

    def update(self, state, action, reward):
        adjusted = reward * (0.5 if action == "neutral" else 1.0)
        super().update(state, action, adjusted)

Macro Agent (context awareness)
class MacroAgent(RLAgent):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__(action_dim=4, reward_weight=0.6)
        self.macro_state = {}

    def act(self, state):
        self.macro_state["last"] = state
        key = str(state)
        if key in self.q_table:
            return max(self.q_table[key], key=self.q_table[key].get)
        if isinstance(state, dict) and "trend" in state:
            return "expand" if state["trend"] > 0 else "contract"
        return "hold"

    def update(self, state, action, reward):
        context_bonus = 0.1 if self.macro_state else 0.0
        super().update(state, action, reward * (1 + context_bonus))
🔥 5. SWARM COORDINATOR (REAL INTELLIGENCE LAYER)

This is the most important part.

class RLSwarm:

    def __init__(self, agents):
        self.agents = agents

    def act(self, state):

        votes = {}
        confidence = {}

        for agent in self.agents:

            action = agent.act(state)

            votes[action] = votes.get(action, 0) + agent.reward_weight

            confidence[action] = confidence.get(action, 0) + 1

        # weighted decision
        best_action = max(votes, key=votes.get)

        return {
            "action": best_action,
            "confidence": confidence[best_action] / len(self.agents)
        }
🧠 6. META POLICY LAYER (ADVANCED UPGRADE)

This is where intelligence becomes adaptive.

class MetaPolicy:

    def __init__(self):
        self.agent_scores = {}

    def update(self, agent_name, reward):

        self.agent_scores[agent_name] = \
            self.agent_scores.get(agent_name, 0) + reward

    def weight(self, agent_name):

        total = sum(self.agent_scores.values()) + 1e-6
        return self.agent_scores.get(agent_name, 0) / total
🔥 7. TRAINING LOOP (REAL RL SWARM LEARNING)
class SwarmTrainer:

    def __init__(self, swarm, environment):
        self.swarm = swarm
        self.env = environment

    def train(self, episodes=1000):

        state = self.env.reset()

        for _ in range(episodes):

            decision = self.swarm.act(state)

            next_state, reward = self.env.step(decision["action"])

            for agent in self.swarm.agents:
                agent.update(state, decision["action"], reward)

            state = next_state

📁 causal/global_graph.py
from collections import defaultdict

class GlobalCausalGraph:

    def __init__(self):
        self.edges = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(float))

    def add(self, cause, effect, weight=1.0):

        self.edges[cause][effect] += weight

    def infer(self, node):

        return self.edges.get(node, {})

🌍 2. WORLD MODEL SIMULATION ENGINE
🔥 Core idea

AARIYA does not predict:

it simulates possible futures

📁 world_model/world_sim.py
import copy
import random

class WorldModel:

    def simulate(self, state, steps=10):

        trajectory = []

        current = copy.deepcopy(state)

        for _ in range(steps):

            current = self._transition(current)

            trajectory.append(current)

        return trajectory

    def _transition(self, state):

        # stochastic world dynamics
        state["market"] += random.uniform(-1, 1)
        state["weather_risk"] = random.random()
        state["behavior_shift"] = random.random()

        return state
🧠 WHY THIS MATTERS

Now AARIYA can:

test futures before acting
compare outcomes
choose optimal timeline

This is model-based RL reasoning.

import random

class AgentEvolution:

    def mutate(self, agent):

        mutation = random.uniform(-0.1, 0.1)

        agent.weight = max(0.1, agent.weight + mutation)

        return agent

class SafetyGovernor:

    def __init__(self):
        self.max_risk = 0.3
        self.max_exposure = 1000

    def validate(self, action):

        if action["risk"] > self.max_risk:
            return False

        if action["exposure"] > self.max_exposure:
            return False

        return True

        return {
            "target": agent.name,
            "change": "adjust_weight",
            "delta": 0.05,
            "reason": "low reward stability improvement"
        }
🧠 2. SAFETY VALIDATOR (CRITICAL LAYER)
📁 evolution/safety_validator.py
class SafetyValidator:

    def validate(self, mutation):

        allowed_changes = [
            "adjust_weight",
            "update_threshold",
            "tune_reward"
        ]

        if mutation["change"] not in allowed_changes:
            return False

        if abs(mutation.get("delta", 0)) > 0.2:
            return False

        return True
🧠 3. SANDBOX TEST ENVIRONMENT
📁 evolution/sandbox.py
class Sandbox:

    def test(self, agent, mutation):

        baseline = agent.performance

        # simulate mutation
        if mutation["change"] == "adjust_weight":
            agent.weight += mutation["delta"]

        return {
            "before": baseline,
            "after": agent.performance
        }
🧠 4. A/B EVALUATION SYSTEM
📁 evolution/ab_test.py
class ABTest:

    def decide(self, result):

        if result["after"] > result["before"]:
            return "APPROVE"

        return "REJECT"
🧠 5. PATCH APPLICATION ENGINE
📁 evolution/apply_patch.py
class PatchApplier:

    def apply(self, agent, mutation):

        if mutation["change"] == "adjust_weight":
            agent.weight += mutation["delta"]

        return agent
🧠 6. FULL SELF-IMPROVEMENT LOOP
📁 evolution/self_improver.py
class SelfImprover:

    def __init__(self, agents, engine, validator, sandbox, abtest, applier):
        self.agents = agents
        self.engine = engine
        self.validator = validator
        self.sandbox = sandbox
        self.abtest = abtest
        self.applier = applier

    def run_cycle(self):

        for agent in self.agents:

            mutation = self.engine.propose(agent)

            if not self.validator.validate(mutation):
                continue

            result = self.sandbox.test(agent, mutation)

            decision = self.abtest.decide(result)

            if decision == "APPROVE":
                self.applier.apply(agent, mutation)
🧠 WHAT THIS ACTUALLY MEANS

✔ Controlled self-modification

AI can improve itself safely

✔ No unsafe code rewriting

Everything is constrained

✔ Performance-driven evolution

Only improvements survive

✔ System stability preserved

No destructive mutations

🧠 1. UNIFIED WORLD MODEL (CORE PREDICTIVE BRAIN)

This replaces “prediction” with:

simulation of multiple possible futures

📁 world_model/unified_world_model.py
import copy
import random

class UnifiedWorldModel:

    def simulate(self, state, steps=10):

        futures = []

        for _ in range(5):  # multiple futures

            s = copy.deepcopy(state)
            trajectory = []

            for _ in range(steps):

                s = self._transition(s)
                trajectory.append(copy.deepcopy(s))

            futures.append(trajectory)

        return futures

    def _transition(self, state):

        state["price"] += random.uniform(-2, 2)
        state["volatility"] = abs(random.random())
        state["macro_risk"] = random.random()

        return state
🧠 RESULT

AARIYA now:

does not predict 1 outcome
simulates multiple futures
compares trajectories

from collections import defaultdict

class CausalMemory:

    def __init__(self):
        self.graph = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(float))

    def update(self, cause, effect, weight=1.0):

        self.graph[cause][effect] += weight

    def query(self, node):

        return self.graph.get(node, {})

from collections import deque

class EventBus:

    def __init__(self):
        self.queue = deque()

    def publish(self, event):
        self.queue.append(event)

    def consume(self):

        if self.queue:
            return self.queue.popleft()

        return {"status": "unavailable"}

class RegionNode:

    def __init__(self, region_name, model):
        self.region = region_name
        self.model = model
        self.local_data = []

    def train_local(self, batch):

        self.local_data.extend(batch)
        self.model.train(batch)

        return self.model.get_weights()
📁 cloud/parameter_server.py
class ParameterServer:

    def __init__(self):
        self.global_weights = {}

    def aggregate(self, node_updates):

        for update in node_updates:

            for k, v in update.items():
                self.global_weights[k] = (
                    self.global_weights.get(k, 0) + v
                ) / 2

        return self.global_weights
📁 cloud/sync_engine.py
class SyncEngine:

    def sync(self, nodes, server):

        updates = []

        for node in nodes:
            updates.append(node.train_local([]))

        return server.aggregate(updates)

locally adaptive

📁 causal/global_memory.py
from collections import defaultdict

class GlobalCausalMemory:

    def __init__(self):
        self.graph = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(float))

    def add_relation(self, cause, effect, weight=1.0):

        self.graph[cause][effect] += weight

    def get_effects(self, cause):

        return self.graph.get(cause, {})

    def get_causes(self, effect):

        return {
            c: effects[effect]
            for c, effects in self.graph.items()
            if effect in effects
        }
🧠 CAUSAL UPDATE ENGINE
class CausalUpdater:

    def update_from_event(self, memory, event):

        cause = event["cause"]
        effect = event["effect"]
        strength = event.get("strength", 1.0)

        memory.add_relation(cause, effect, strength)

📁 compiler/architecture_graph.py
class ArchitectureGraph:

    def __init__(self):
        self.nodes = {}
        self.edges = []

    def add_node(self, name, component_type):
        self.nodes[name] = component_type

    def add_edge(self, src, dst, relation):
        self.edges.append((src, dst, relation))

    def get_snapshot(self):
        return {
            "nodes": self.nodes,
            "edges": self.edges
        }
🧠 2. IMPROVEMENT PROPOSAL ENGINE (AI BRAIN)

📁 compiler/proposer.py
class ArchitectureProposer:

    def propose(self, graph_metrics):

        proposals = []

        if graph_metrics["latency"] > 200:
            proposals.append({
                "action": "add_cache_layer",
                "target": "inference_pipeline"
            })

        if graph_metrics["accuracy"] < 0.7:
            proposals.append({
                "action": "upgrade_model",
                "target": "prediction_engine"
            })

        return proposals
🧠 3. ARCHITECTURE COMPILER (CORE ENGINE)

This converts proposals → executable system changes

📁 compiler/compiler.py
class ArchitectureCompiler:

    def compile(self, graph, proposals):

        for p in proposals:

            if p["action"] == "add_cache_layer":

                graph.add_node("cache_layer", "optimization")
                graph.add_edge("cache_layer", p["target"], "improves")

            if p["action"] == "upgrade_model":

                graph.add_node("new_model_v2", "ml_model")
                graph.add_edge("new_model_v2", p["target"], "replaces")

        return graph
🧠 4. SANDBOX SIMULATOR (CRITICAL SAFETY LAYER)

Nothing is applied directly.

📁 compiler/sandbox.py
import copy

class Sandbox:

    def simulate(self, graph):

        test_graph = copy.deepcopy(graph)

        # simulate performance impact
        score = len(test_graph.nodes) * 0.01

        return {
            "simulated_latency": 100 - score * 10,
            "simulated_accuracy": 0.75 + score * 0.1
        }

class Evaluator:

    def evaluate(self, metrics):

        if metrics["simulated_accuracy"] < 0.6:
            return False

        if metrics["simulated_latency"] > 250:
            return False

        return True

class SafetyGovernor:

    def approve(self, proposal):

        forbidden = ["self_delete", "unbounded_loop", "disable_risk"]

        if proposal.get("action") in forbidden:
            return False

        return True
🔁 7. FULL SELF-MODIFYING LOOP
📁 compiler/smac_loop.py
class SMACLoop:

    def __init__(self, graph, proposer, compiler, sandbox, evaluator, safety):

        self.graph = graph
        self.proposer = proposer
        self.compiler = compiler
        self.sandbox = sandbox
        self.evaluator = evaluator
        self.safety = safety

    def run_cycle(self, metrics):

        proposals = self.proposer.propose(metrics)

        safe_proposals = [
            p for p in proposals if self.safety.approve(p)
        ]

        new_graph = self.compiler.compile(self.graph, safe_proposals)

        sim = self.sandbox.simulate(new_graph)

        if self.evaluator.evaluate(sim):

            self.graph = new_graph
            return "DEPLOYED"

        return "REJECTED"

class WorldModel:

        return "aggressive"
class Worker:

    def act(self, strategy):
        return "buy" if strategy == "aggressive" else "hold"

import random

class ModelGenerator:

    def generate(self):

        return {
            "layers": random.choice([2, 3, 4]),
            "hidden": random.choice([32, 64, 128]),
            "activation": "relu"
        }
📁 2. MODEL BUILDER
import torch
import torch.nn as nn

class DynamicModel(nn.Module):

    def __init__(self, config):
        super().__init__()

        layers = []

        input_dim = 10

        for _ in range(config["layers"]):

            layers.append(nn.Linear(input_dim, config["hidden"]))
            layers.append(nn.ReLU())
            input_dim = config["hidden"]

        layers.append(nn.Linear(input_dim, 1))

        self.net = nn.Sequential(*layers)

    def forward(self, x):
        return self.net(x)
📁 3. TRAIN + EVALUATE
class Trainer:

    def train(self, model, data):

        return self._compute_confidence_score(prediction, evidence)
📁 4. SELECTION ENGINE
class Selector:

    def select(self, models_scores):

        return max(models_scores, key=lambda x: x["score"])

for config in model_space:

    model = build(config)

    score = train(model)

    store(config, score)

class AutopilotOrchestrator:

    def route(self, event):

        if event["type"] == "market_update":
            return "intelligence_core"

        if event["type"] == "drift_detected":
            return "retraining_pipeline"

        if event["type"] == "system_load_high":
            return "autoscaler"

        return "logger"

        if metrics["cpu"] < 30:
            return "scale_down"

        return "stable"

# agents/executor.py


import subprocess


class ExecutorAgent:


    async def execute(self, step):


        action = step.get("action")


        if action == "bash":
            return self.run_bash(step["command"])


        elif action == "python":
            return self.run_python(step["code"])


        elif action == "web_search":
            return self.web_search(step["query"])


        return {
            "status": "unknown_action"
        }


    def run_bash(self, command):


        result = subprocess.run(
            command,
            shell=True,
            capture_output=True,
            text=True
        )


        return {
            "stdout": result.stdout,
            "stderr": result.stderr
        }

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  6. Local LLM Router
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Supports multiple models.

# llm/router.py


import aiohttp


OLLAMA_URL = "http://localhost:11434/api/generate"


async def ask_llm(prompt, model="deepseek-r1"):


    payload = {
        "model": model,
        "prompt": prompt,
        "stream": False
    }


    async with aiohttp.ClientSession() as session:
        async with session.post(
            OLLAMA_URL,
            json=payload
        ) as response:


            data = await response.json()
            return data["response"]

  3. Prompt Optimization Engine
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Aariya improves prompts automatically.

# optimization/prompt_optimizer.py

class PromptOptimizer:

    def improve_prompt(
        self,
        old_prompt,
        feedback
    ):

        improvements = []

        if "too vague" in feedback:
            improvements.append(
                "Add explicit constraints"
            )

        if "hallucination" in feedback:
            improvements.append(
                "Add verification step"
            )

        return {
            "original": old_prompt,
            "improvements": improvements
        }

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  5. Safe Self-Rewriting System
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Never allow unrestricted self-modification.

Use:

sandboxing
validation
rollback
versioning
# self_improvement/rewrite_engine.py

import shutil
import os

class RewriteEngine:

    def backup(self, path):

        shutil.copytree(
            path,
            f"{path}_backup"
        )

    def apply_patch(
        self,
        file_path,
        new_code
    ):

        with open(file_path, "w") as f:
            f.write(new_code)

    def rollback(self, path):

        backup = f"{path}_backup"

        if os.path.exists(backup):
            shutil.copytree(
                backup,
                path,
                dirs_exist_ok=True
            )

class Simulator:

    def simulate(
        self,
        action,
        world_state
    ):

        predictions = []

        if action == "deploy_update":

            predictions.append({
                "outcome": "possible downtime",
                "probability": 0.32
            })

            predictions.append({
                "outcome": "performance increase",
                "probability": 0.71
            })

        return predictions

class PrioritySystem:
